<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
We assume knowledge of $p$ in \eqref{eq:unb-est} to establish the theoretical guarantees, akin to \cite{JalaliWillett18,loh2011high}. In practice, when $p$ is unknown we can use a hyperparameter tuning approach by varying $p$ and testing the recovered matrix on a downstream prediction task.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:unb-est}
If $p > 0$ then
 
\begin{align*}
\underset{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}}{\operatorname{argmin}} \ \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \left[ g(A) \right] = \underset{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}}{\operatorname{argmin}} \ \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \left[\big\|{\tilde{X}-AX}\big\|_F^2 \right].
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:talgrand-gamma}
Let $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},d)$ be a metric space. A sequence of subsets of $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}$, $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_r)_{r \ge 0}$, is an admissible sequence if $\ensuremath{\left | \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_0 \right |} = 1$ and $\ensuremath{\left | \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_r \right |} \le 2^{2^r}$ for all $r \ge 1$. Then for any $0 < \alpha < \infty$, the $\ensuremath{\gamma_{\alpha}}$ function of $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},d)$ is defined as
\begin{equation*}
    \ensuremath{\gamma_{\alpha}} (\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},d) \coloneq \inf \sup_{s\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}} \sum_{r=0}^{\infty} 2^{r/\alpha} d(s,\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_r),
\end{equation*}
where the $\inf$ is over the admissible sequences of $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def:tal_func}
Given a set $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}} \subset \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^{n \times m}$, the Gaussian width $w(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}})$ of $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}$ is given by
\begin{equation*}
    w(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}) \coloneq \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \sup_{s\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}} \Big\langleG,s\Big\rangle,
\end{equation*}
where $G$ has i.i.d. standard Gaussian entries.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
For a convex set $\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$, the tangent cone of $\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$ at $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$ is defined as the set
 
\begin{equation*}
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}},A} := \operatorname{cl}\{t(B-A):t\ge 0, B\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\},
\end{equation*}
 
where $\operatorname{cl}(\cdot)$ denotes the closure operation.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:main-thm}
There exist constants $c_1, c_2  > 1$ such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$ and $B \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$, if
 
 
\begin{align*}
T \ge \frac{c_1 J^4(A^*) \max \left\{{ \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F),\log^2 \left(1/\delta\right)}\right\}}{p^4},
\end{align*}
then it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$,
\begin{align*}
&\big\|{\widehat{A}-A^*}\big\|_F \le 
 c_2 \bigg[\frac{J^3(A^*)}{p^2} \bigg(\frac{\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)}{T}+\frac{\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)}{\sqrt{T}}\\
 &+\sqrt{\frac{\log \left(1/\delta\right)}{T}}\bigg)
+ \frac{J^2(A^*)}{p}\big\|{A^*-B}\big\|_F \bigg]. 
\end{align*}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
When $p=1$, Theorem \ref{thm:main-thm} reduces to \cite[Theorem 1]{tyagi2024learning}, up to a $J(A^*)$ factor, and under slightly stronger conditions on $T$.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:pgd-thm}
Suppose that the learning rate is $\gamma = \frac{1}{2p^2 T J^2(A^*) }.$ There exist constants $c, C, C_1, C_2 > 1$ such that for any $B \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$ and $\delta \in (0,1)$, if
     
     
    \begin{align}
        T &\geq \frac{CJ^4(A^*)}{p^4}\left\{\gamma_2^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{.}\big\|_F) + \log^2(1/\delta) \right\} \nonumber 
        \\
        &+ \frac{C J^2(A^*)}{p^2}\left\{\gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2) + \log(1/\delta)\right\}, \label{eq:T-PGD-cond}
    \end{align}
then there exists { $\rho \leq 1-\frac{1}{c J^2(A^*)}$} such that with probability at least $1 - \delta$, it holds for all $k = 0,1,\dots$ that
 
 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:pgd-thm-final-form}
    \big\|{A^{(k)} - A^*}\big\|_F \leq \rho^k \big\|{A^{(0)} - A^*}\big\|_F  
    + \frac{\varepsilon}{1 - \rho}.
\end{equation}
Here $\varepsilon$ is given by
\begin{align*}
    \varepsilon &= \varepsilon_T =
     \frac{C_1 J(A^*)}{p^2}\Bigg[\frac{\gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2)}{T} + \frac{\gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{.}\big\|_F)}{\sqrt{T}} \\
     &+ \sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{T}} \Bigg] + \frac{C_2\big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F}{p}.
\end{align*}
Since $(1-\rho)^{-1} = O(J^2(A^*))$, $\varepsilon/(1-\rho)$ is of the same order as the statistical error in Theorem~\ref{thm:main-thm}.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
$\gamma$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:pgd-thm} depends on $J(A^*)$ which is an unknown parameter defined on $A^*$. In practice, one can replace $J(A^*)$ by a known upper bound $\xi$, with no change in the PGD convergence rates.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rem:utility_thms}
 Theorems \ref{thm:main-thm} and \ref{thm:pgd-thm} are complementary. Theorem \ref{thm:main-thm} gives statistical rates for the global minimizer of \eqref{eq:unb-est}, while Theorem \ref{thm:pgd-thm} shows that the iterates of PGD cluster in a small ball around $A^*$. Using triangle inequality, clearly $\big\|{\widehat{A} - A^{(k)}}\big\|_F \lesssim \varepsilon$ under \eqref{eq:T-PGD-cond}.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{rem:p_scaling}
    In Theorem~\ref{thm:main-thm}, for a fixed level $\delta$, we have $\big\|{\widehat A - A^*}\big\|_F = o(1)$ even as $p = o(1)$, under the sufficient condition
    \begin{align}\label{eq:p-floor-thm1}
&J^{3/2} (A^*)\Bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_2)}{T}} + \frac{\sqrt{\gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_F)}}{T^{1/4}}\nonumber \\ &+ \sqrt{\big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F} \Bigg)  = o(p), \quad \text{with} \quad p = o(1).
    \end{align}
For Theorem \ref{thm:pgd-thm}, if $\big\|{A^{(0)} - A^*}\big\|_F = O(1)$, $p = o(1)$, and \eqref{eq:p-floor-thm1} holds, then for $k = k_T \gtrsim \log\left(1 / (\big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F + \varepsilon_T)\right)$ iterations, we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{A^{(k_T)} - A^*}\big\|_F = O(\big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F + \varepsilon_T) = o(1).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
\label{rem:sparse_case}
While Theorem~\ref{thm:main-thm} holds for general convex sets $\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$, the bounds can be sub-optimal in some settings, such as $\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}} = \left\{{A \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^{n \times n}: \big\|{\operatorname{vec}(A)}\big\|_{1} \le \big\|{\operatorname{vec}(A^*)}\big\|_{1}}\right\}$, where $A^*$ is $k$-sparse, as bounding $\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}$ is challenging (as noted in Remark 3 of \cite{tyagi2024learning}). Achieving optimal guarantees in this setting requires specialized analysis exploiting the properties of $A^*$ and the $\ell_1$-ball.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
\label{rem:pgd_proof_comp}
    The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:pgd-thm} follows a similar approach to
    \cite[Theorem 1]{lv2021}. While their result is stated for  specific convex sets with a convex objective, the techniques used directly apply to general convex sets. We extend their ideas to establish convergence guarantees for PGD for the (potentially non-convex) program \eqref{eq:unb-est}.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:sub-stat}
There exist constants $c_1,c_2>1$ such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, if $T \geq c_1\frac{J^4(A^*)\max\left\{{d,\log^2 \left(1/\delta\right)}\right\}}{p^4}$ it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$,
\[
\big\|{\widehat{A}-A^*}\big\|_F \leq c_2 \frac{J^3(A^*)}{p^2}  \bigg(\frac{\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{\log \left(1/\delta\right)}}{\sqrt{T}}\bigg).
\]
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:main-thm} with $B=A^*$, and the bounds $\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}} \cap \mathbb{S}_n, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \lesssim d$ and $\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}} \cap \mathbb{S}_n, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \lesssim \sqrt{d}$; see e.g., \cite{tyagi2024learning}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:sub-pgd}
                                                                       
Suppose that $\gamma = \frac{1}{2p^2 T J^2(A^*) }$. Then there exist constants $c, C_1,C_2>1$ such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, if
 
                  
 $       T \geq C_1\frac{J^4(A^*)\left(d + \log^2(1/\delta)\right)}{p^4},$
                
 
then with probability at least $1-\delta$, it holds for all $k=0,1,\dots$,
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{A^{(k)}-A^*}\big\|_F \le \rho^k \big\|{A^{(0)}-A^*}\big\|_F+\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\rho_{}},
\end{equation*}
where $\rho \le 1-\frac{1}{cJ^2(A^*)}$ and

  
\begin{equation*}
\varepsilon = C_2 \frac{J(A^*)}{p^2}\left[
                            
\frac{\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{T}} \right].
\end{equation*}
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Use Theorem~\ref{thm:pgd-thm} with $B=A^*$, and  bounds on $\gamma_1, \gamma_2$ from the proof of Corollary \ref{cor:sub-stat}.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
For $p = 1$, Corollary \ref{cor:sub-stat} recovers the statement of  \cite[Corollary 1]{tyagi2024learning} up to constants and a $J^2(A^*)$ factor, and matches the error bound in \cite[Theorem 3(iii)]{zheng2021finite} up to an additional $\log(1/\delta)$ term.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
In Corollary \ref{cor:sub-stat}, for a fixed level $\delta$, we can have $\big\|{\hat{A}-A^*}\big\|_F = o(1)$ with $p = o(1)$, as long as 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:p-floor-subspace}
    J^{3/2}(A^*)d^{1/4}T^{-1/4} = o(p).
\end{equation}
Moreover, if $\big\|{A^{(0)} - A^*}\big\|_F = O(1)$, $p = o(1)$, and \eqref{eq:p-floor-subspace} holds, then with $k = k_T \gtrsim \log(1/\varepsilon_T)$ iterations,
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{A^{(k_T)} - A^*}\big\|_F = O(\varepsilon_T) = o(1).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
When $\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$ is spanned by $d$ orthonormal matrices, as considered in the experiments, the computational and space complexities of the projection step are $O(n^2 d)$ (due to computation of $d$ inner products).
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:isotone-stat}
There exist constants $c_1,c_2 > 1$ such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, if 
\begin{equation*}
    T \geq c_1 \frac{J^4(A^*)\max\left\{k(A^*)\log^8(n), \log^2(1/\delta) \right\}}{p^4},
\end{equation*}
where $k(A^*)$ is the minimum number of rectangular blocks for which $A^*$ is constant, then with probability at least $1 - \delta$, it holds that 
\begin{align*}
    \big\|{\widehat A - A^*}\big\|_F \leq c_2 &\frac{J^3(A^*)}{p^2}\Bigg(\frac{n^{3/4}\sqrt{k(A^*)}\log^4(n)}{T}\\ &+\frac{\sqrt{k(A^*)}\log^4(n)+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{T}}\Bigg).
\end{align*}
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Substitute the bound on $\gamma_1\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2\right)$ from Lemma \ref{lem:isotonic-gamma1-bound}, and on $\gamma_2\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}, \big\|{.}\big\|_F \right)$ from Lemma \ref{lem:gamma2-isotone-bound}, into Theorem \ref{thm:main-thm} with $B = A^*$. 

To bound $\gamma_2\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}, \big\|{.}\big\|_F \right)$, we first relate it to the statistical dimension of $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}, A^*}$ through the Gaussian width, by  \cite[Prop. 10.2]{tropp2014} and Talagrand's majorizing measures theorem. Then, the statistical dimension is bounded using equation (4.4) of \cite{chatterjee2018}.
 
To bound $\gamma_1\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_A^*, \big\|{.}\big\|_2\right)$, we use the usual metric entropy bounds, splitting at a threshold $r$ representing the fine and coarse scales. The statistical dimension bound is used for the coarse scale. At the fine scale, we rely on the sharp entropy number estimate in \cite{hinrichs2017} to obtain a tighter metric entropy bound.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:isotone-pgd}
Suppose $\gamma = \frac{1}{2p^2 T J^2(A^*) }$.
There exist constants $c, C_1, C_2 > 1$ such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, if
 
    \begin{align}
        T &\geq C_1\frac{J^4(A^*)}{p^4}\left\{k(A^*)\log^8(n) + \log^2(1/\delta) \right\} \nonumber
        \\&+ C_1 \frac{J^2(A^*)}{p^2}\left\{n^{3/4}\sqrt{k(A^*)}\log^4(n) + \log(1/\delta)\right\},
    \end{align}
then with probability at least $1 - \delta$, it holds for all $k = 0,1,\dots$ that
 
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{A^{(k)} - A^*}\big\|_F \leq \rho^k \big\|{A^{(0)} - A^*}\big\|_F + \frac{\varepsilon}{1 - \rho},
\end{equation*}
where $\rho \leq 1-\frac{1}{cJ^2(A^*)}$ and
\begin{align*}
    \varepsilon = C_2\frac{J(A^*)}{p^2}\Bigg(\frac{n^{3/4}\sqrt{k(A^*)}\log^4(n)}{T} \\ + \frac{\sqrt{k(A^*)}\log^4(n)+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{T}} \Bigg).
\end{align*}
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Apply bounds on $\gamma_{1}, \gamma_2$ from Lemmas \ref{lem:isotonic-gamma1-bound} and \ref{lem:gamma2-isotone-bound} into Theorem \ref{thm:pgd-thm}, and use $B = A^*$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
For a fixed level $\delta$, the condition for $\big\|{\widehat A - A^*}\big\|_F = o(1)$ with $p = o(1)$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:p-floor-isotone}
J^{3/2}(A^*)k^{1/4}(A^*)\log^2(n)\max\left\{\frac{n^{3/8}}{\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{T^{1/4}} \right\} = o(p).
\end{equation}
Moreover, if $\big\|{A^{(0)} - A^*}\big\|_F = O(1)$, $p = o(1)$, and \eqref{eq:p-floor-isotone} holds, then with $k = k_T \gtrsim \log(1/\varepsilon_T)$ iterations,
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{A^{k_T} - A^*}\big\|_F = O(\varepsilon_T) = o(1).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:gamma2-isotone-bound}
    Consider the class of bi-isotonic matrices. There exists an constant $C > 0$ such that
    \begin{equation*}
        \gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}, \big\|{.}\big\|_F) \leq C\sqrt{k(A^*)}\log^4(n).
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:isotonic-gamma1-bound}
    For the class of bi-isotonic matrices, there exists an absolute constant $C > 0$ such that
    \begin{equation*}
         \gamma_1\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2 \right) \leq Cn^{3/4}\sqrt{k(A^*)}\log^4(n).
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
The projection step for bi-isotonic matrices can be performed by Dykstra’s projection algorithm \citep{dykstra1985}, with a computational complexity of $O(n^2 k)$, where $k$ is the number of iterations.
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:psd-sup}
Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}$ be a set of matrices and $\nu$ be a vector with independent, zero-mean, $L$-subgaussian entries with variance 1. Let,
\begin{align*}
F &\coloneq \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right) \left[\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)+d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}})\right]+d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}})d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}),\\
V &\coloneq d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}})\left[\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)+d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}})\right], \text{ and, } U \coloneq d^2_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}})
\end{align*}
There exist constants $c_1,c_2>0$ depending only on $L$ such that for any $t > 0$,
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\sup_{M \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}} \ensuremath{\left | \big\|{M\nu}\big\|_2^2-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \left[\big\|{M\nu}\big\|_2^2\right] \right |} \ge c_1 F+t\right) 
\le 2\exp \left(-c_2\min\left\{{\frac{t^2}{V},\frac{t}{U}}\right\}\right).
\end{align*}
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:non-psd-sup}
Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}$ be a set of matrices and $\nu$ be a vector with independent, zero-mean, $1$-subgaussian entries. For $M\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}$, let $C_M(\nu) = \nu^\top M \nu - \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \left[\nu^\top M \nu\right]$. Then,
\begin{enumerate}
\item There exists a universal constant $c>0$ such that for any $l \ge 1$,
\begin{align*}
\left(\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \sup_{M \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}} \ensuremath{\left | C_M(\nu) \right |}^l\right)^{1/l} \le
 c \bigg(\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)+\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{l}d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}})+ld_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}})\bigg).
\end{align*}
\item There exists a universal constant $c'>0$ such that for any $u \ge 1$,
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \bigg(\sup_{M \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}} \ensuremath{\left | C_M(\nu) \right |} \ge L\bigg) \le e^{-u}.
\end{align*}
where 
\begin{align*}
L=c'\bigg(&\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)+\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right) 
+\sqrt{u}d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}})+ud_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}})\bigg).
\end{align*}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:bounded-func-conc}
Consider $X = (X_1,X_2,\dots,X_n)$ independent random variables with $X_i \in [a,b]$ almost surely. Let $f:\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^{n} \rightarrow \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ be convex and $L$-Lipschitz with respect to $\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2$ norm. Then $\forall t \ge 0$
\[\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(|f(X)-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} f(X)|\ge t\right) \le 2\exp \left(\frac{-t^2}{2L^2(b-a)^2}\right).\]
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let $\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P}[\cdot]$ denote the expected value over $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$, conditioned on $(x_t)_{t=1}^T$. Consider,
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\widetilde{Z}-AZ}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{A \cdot \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top)}\big\|_F^2.
\end{align*}
We will use $g(A)=\big\|{\widetilde{Z}-AZ}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{A \cdot \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top)}\big\|_F^2$ in the rest of the proof for ease of notation. Note that,
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\widetilde{Z}-AZ}\big\|_F^2 = \big\|{\widetilde{Z}}\big\|_F^2+\big\|{AZ}\big\|_F^2-2\Big\langle\widetilde{Z},AZ\Big\rangle.
\end{align*}
Let us first consider $\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\left[\big\|{AZ}\big\|_F^2\right]$ and $\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\Big\langle\widetilde{Z},AZ\Big\rangle\right]$. We have,
 
 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\big\|{AZ}\big\|_F^2\right] &= \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\operatorname{Tr}\left(ZZ^\top A^\top A\right)\right] = \operatorname{Tr}\left(\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[ZZ^\top\right] A^\top A\right)\\
&= \operatorname{Tr}\left(\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} P_t y_t y_t^\top {P_t}^\top \right] A^\top A\right)\\
&= \operatorname{Tr}\left(\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} P_t x_t x_t^\top P_t^\top \right] A^\top A\right).
\end{align*}
                                                                                                     
Since ${(P_t)}_{ii}\sim B(p)$ for all $t \in [T]$,
\begin{equation*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} P_t x_t x_t^\top P_t^\top \right] =  \sum_{t=1}^{T-1} S \odot \left( x_t x_t^\top \right)
=  S \odot \sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \left( x_t x_t^\top \right) = S \odot (XX^\top),
\end{equation*}
where $S = p^2 \mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top + p(1-p)I,$ where $\mathbf{1} = (1, \dots, 1)^\top \in \mathbb{R}^n$.

Similarly for $\Big\langle\widetilde{Z},AZ\Big\rangle$,
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\Big\langle\widetilde{Z},AZ\Big\rangle\right] 
&= \operatorname{Tr}\left(\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} P_t x_t x_{t+1}^\top P_{t+1}^\top \right] A\right).
\end{align*}


Since $P_t$ and $P_{t+1}$ are independent,
\begin{equation*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} P_t x_t x_{t+1}^\top P_{t+1}^\top \right] =  \sum_{t=1}^{T-1} p^2 \left( x_t x_{t+1}^\top \right) = p^2 X\widetilde{X}^\top.
\end{equation*}
For simplicity, let $\mu = \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\left[\big\|{\widetilde{Z}}\big\|_F^2\right]$. Note that $\mu$ is independent of $A$. Therefore,
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\big\|{\widetilde{Z}-AZ}\big\|_F^2\right] = \mu+\operatorname{Tr} \left(\left\{S \odot XX^\top\right\} A^\top A\right)-2p^2\operatorname{Tr} \left(X\widetilde{X}^\top A\right).
\end{align*}

Next consider, $\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\big\|{A \cdot \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2\right]$. Note that,
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\big\|{A \cdot \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2\right] & =\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\operatorname{Tr} \left(\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(ZZ^\top\right) A^\top A \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(ZZ^\top\right)\right)\right]\\
&=\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\operatorname{Tr} \left(\operatorname{diag}\left(ZZ^\top\right) A^\top A \right)\right]\\
&=\operatorname{Tr} \left(\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\left[\operatorname{diag}\left(ZZ^\top\right)\right] A^\top A \right)\\
&=p\operatorname{Tr}\left(\operatorname{diag}\left(XX^\top\right) A^\top A \right).
\end{align*}
 
Hence we obtain
 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[g(A)\right] &= \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\big\|{\widetilde{Z}-AZ}\big\|_F^2\right]-(1-p)\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[\big\|{A \cdot \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top)}\big\|_F^2\right]\\
&=\mu+\operatorname{Tr} \left(S \odot XX^\top A^\top A\right)-2p^2\operatorname{Tr} \left(X\widetilde{X}^\top A\right)-(1-p)p\operatorname{Tr}\left(\operatorname{diag}\left(XX^\top\right) A^\top A \right)\\
&=\mu+p^2 \operatorname{Tr} \left(XX^\top A^\top A\right)-2p^2\operatorname{Tr} \left(X\widetilde{X}^\top A\right)\\
&=\mu + p^2 \left(\big\|{AX}\big\|_F^2-2\Big\langle\widetilde{X},AX\Big\rangle+\big\|{\widetilde{X}}\big\|_F^2\right)-p^2\big\|{\widetilde{X}}\big\|_F^2\\
&=\mu'+p^2 \big\|{\widetilde{X}-AX}\big\|_F^2,
\end{align*}
 
where $\mu'=\mu-p^2\big\|{\widetilde{X}}\big\|_F^2$ (which does not depend on $A$). Therefore, we can see that,
 
\begin{align*}
\underset{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}}{\operatorname{argmin}}  \left\{{\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{(x_t)_{t=1}^{T}}\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{P} \left[g(A)\right]}\right\} &= \underset{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}}{\operatorname{argmin}} \left\{{\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{(x_t)_{t=1}^{T}}\mu'+p^2 \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{(x_t)_{t=1}^{T}}\big\|{\widetilde{X}-AX}\big\|_F^2}\right\}\\
&=\underset{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}}{\operatorname{argmin}}\left\{{\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{(x_t)_{t=1}^{T}} \big\|{\widetilde{X}-AX}\big\|_F^2}\right\}\\
&=\underset{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}}{\operatorname{argmin}}\left\{{\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}_{(x_t)_{t=1}^{T}} \sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \big\|{x_{t+1}-Ax_{t}}\big\|_2^2}\right\}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:B-bound}
For any $B \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$, the solution $\widehat{A} \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$ to (\ref{eq:unb-est}) satisfies,
\begin{align}\label{eq:B-bound}
\big\|{(B-\widehat{A})Z}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)D}\big\|_F^2&\le 2\Big\langle\widetilde{Z}-A^*Z,(\widehat{A}-B)Z\Big\rangle +2\big\|{\left(A^*-B\right)Z}\big\|_F\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)Z}\big\|_F\\
&+2(1-p)\Big\langleA^*  D,(\widehat{A}-B) D\Big\rangle+2(1-p)\big\|{(A^*-B)D}\big\|_F \big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)D}\big\|_F \nonumber.
\end{align}
where $D=\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top)$.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:B-bound} follows from considering the optimality of $\widehat{A}$ and the feasibility of $B$, which implies $g(\widehat{A}) \le g(B)$, and then rewriting and reordering some terms. The details are available in Appendix~\ref{appsec:main-inequal}.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:LHS-bound}
There exist constants $c_1, C > 1$ such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$ it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$,
 
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{(B-\widehat{A})Z}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)D}\big\|_F^2 \ge &\Bigg[p^2 T -c_1 \bigg(J^2(A^*)\bar{L}_1+ p(1-p)J^2(A^*)\bar{L}_1\\ 
&+pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)+\log (1/\delta)\right)\bigg) \Bigg] \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2
\end{align*}
 
where 
 
\begin{align*}
\bar{L}_1 &= C \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T} \log \left(1/\delta\right)\right),
\end{align*}
and $D=\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top)$.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:RHS-bound}
There exist constants $c_1, C > 1$ such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$ it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$ that,
\begin{align*}
&2\Big\langle\widetilde{Z}-A^*Z,(\widehat{A}-B)Z\Big\rangle+2\big\|{\left(A^*-B\right)Z}\big\|_F\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)Z}\big\|_F \\
&+2(1-p)\Big\langleA^*  D,(\widehat{A}-B)D\Big\rangle+2(1-p)\big\|{(A^*-B)D}\big\|_F \big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)D}\big\|_F\\
& \le  c_1 \bigg(J^3(A^*)\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F \bigg(\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T\log \left(1/\delta\right)}+\log (1/\delta)\bigg)\\
&+J^2(A^*) \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F\big\|{A^*-B}\big\|_F \bigg(p T+\bar{L}_1 +p(1-p)^2 \bar{L}_1+p\sqrt{T} \bigg(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg).
\end{align*}
where $D=\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top)$, and,
\begin{align*}
\bar{L}_1 &= C \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T} \log \left(1/\delta\right)\right).
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Consider the optimal solution $\widehat{A} \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$ that minimizes $g(A)$ in \eqref{eq:unb-est}, and any $B \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$. Since $B$ is  feasible, hence
 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:feas-opt-B}
\big\|{\widetilde{Z}-\widehat{A}Z}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{\widehat{A}  D}\big\|_F^2 \le \big\|{\widetilde{Z}-BZ}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{B  D}\big\|_F^2.
\end{equation}

Intercalating by $B$, the LHS of \eqref{eq:feas-opt-B} can be written as
 
\begin{align*}
&\big\|{\widetilde{Z}-\widehat{A}Z}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{\widehat{A}  D}\big\|_F^2 = \big\|{\widetilde{Z}-BZ+(B-\widehat{A})Z}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{B  D+(\widehat A-B)D}\big\|_F^2 \\
&= \left( \big\|{\widetilde{Z}-BZ}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{B  D}\big\|_F^2\right)+\big\|{(B-\widehat{A})Z}\big\|_F^2+2\Big\langle\widetilde{Z}-BZ,(B-\widehat{A})Z\Big\rangle\\
&-(1-p)\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)D}\big\|_F^2-2(1-p)\Big\langleB  D,(\widehat{A}-B)D\Big\rangle.
\end{align*}
 
Substituting the above into ~\eqref{eq:feas-opt-B}, and further intercalating by $A^*$, we have
 
\begin{align}\label{eq:prelim-AB-ineq}
&\big\|{(B-\widehat{A})Z}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)D}\big\|_F^2 \le 2\Big\langle\widetilde{Z}-BZ,(\widehat{A}-B)Z\Big\rangle+2(1-p)\Big\langleB  D,(\widehat{A}-B)D\Big\rangle\\
&\le 2\Big\langle\widetilde{Z}-A^*Z,(\widehat{A}-B)Z\Big\rangle+2(1-p)\Big\langleA^*  D,(\widehat{A}-B)D\Big\rangle\nonumber\\
&+2\Big\langle\left(A^*-B\right)Z,(\widehat{A}-B)Z\Big\rangle-2(1-p)\Big\langle\left(A^*-B \right) D,(\widehat{A}-B)D\Big\rangle\nonumber.
\end{align}

From the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have
 
\begin{align*}
\Big\langle\left(A^*-B\right)Z,(\widehat{A}-B)Z\Big\rangle &\le \big\|{\left(A^*-B\right)Z}\big\|_F\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)Z}\big\|_F, \\
 
-\Big\langle\left(A^*-B \right) D,(\widehat{A}-B)D\Big\rangle 
&\le \big\|{(A^*-B)D}\big\|_F \big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)D}\big\|_F.
\end{align*}
 
 
Combining this with ~\eqref{eq:prelim-AB-ineq} yields
 
 
\begin{align*}
&\big\|{(B-\widehat{A})Z}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)D}\big\|_F^2 \le 2\Big\langle\widetilde{Z}-A^*Z,(\widehat{A}-B)Z\Big\rangle+2(1-p)\Big\langleA^*  D,(\widehat{A}-B)D\Big\rangle\\
&+2(1-p)\big\|{(A^*-B)D}\big\|_F \big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)D}\big\|_F +2\big\|{\left(A^*-B\right)Z}\big\|_F\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)Z}\big\|_F.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
In order to prove Lemma~\ref{lem:LHS-bound}, we start by observing the following. 

\begin{claim}\label{clm:quad-frob-norm}
Let $\breve{P} = \ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} P_1 & 0 & \dots & 0\\ 0 & P_2 & \dots & 0\\\vdots & \vdots & \dots & \vdots\\ 0 & 0 & \dots & P_{T-1} \end{bmatrix}}$, and $\bar{A} = \frac{\widehat{A}-B}{\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F}$. Then,
 
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{\left(\widehat{A}-B\right)Z}\big\|_F^2= \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2  \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \left(\breve{P} \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\breve{P}\right)\Gamma \eta,
\end{equation*}
and 
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{\left(\widehat{A}-B\right)\operatorname{diag}^{1/2} \left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2=\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2  \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \left(\breve{P} \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\right)\Gamma \eta.
\end{equation*}
\end{claim}
The proof of Claim~\ref{clm:quad-frob-norm} is deferred to Appendix~\ref{appsec:clm-quad-frob-norm}. Letting $\bar{A} = \frac{\widehat{A}-B}{\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F}$, Claim~\ref{clm:quad-frob-norm} implies, 
\begin{align*}
&\big\|{\bar{A} Z}\big\|_F^2= \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \left(\breve{P} \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\breve{P}\right)\Gamma \eta = \big\|{\left(\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\breve{P}\right)\Gamma \eta}\big\|_2^2,\\ 
&\big\|{\bar{A}\operatorname{diag}^{1/2} \left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2=\eta^\top \Gamma^\top \left(\breve{P} \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right) \breve{P}\right)\Gamma \eta = \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right) \breve{P}\Gamma \eta}\big\|_2^2.
\end{align*}
Therefore, we can see that,
\begin{align*}
&\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)Z}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{(\widehat{A}-B)\operatorname{diag}^{1/2} \left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2 \\
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2 \bigg(\big\|{\bar{A} Z}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{\bar{A}\operatorname{diag}^{1/2} \left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2\bigg)\\
&= \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2 \bigg(\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right) \breve{P}\Gamma \eta}\big\|_2^2-(1-p)\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right) \breve{P}\Gamma \eta}\big\|_2^2\bigg).
\end{align*}
 
 
A lower bound on this can be achieved by first establishing a lower bound on $\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A} \right) \breve{P}\Gamma \eta}\big\|_2^2$, and then an upper bound on $\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right) \breve{P}\Gamma \eta}\big\|_2^2$. In the standard full-observation setting, this can be achieved through a careful application of Theorem~\ref{thm:psd-sup} on the quadratics formed in Claim~\ref{clm:quad-frob-norm}, since the entries of $\eta$ are mean $0$, variance $1$ and subgaussian. However, in our setting, a direct application of Theorem~\ref{thm:psd-sup} does not work as we have randomness over $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$ as well as $\eta$. 

\@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@}{1.5ex plus
   0.5ex minus .2ex}{-1em}{\normalsize\bfseries}{Deriving bounds conditional on $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$.} We start by stating two key lemmas that enable us to bound the $\big\|{AZ}\big\|_F^2-(1-p)\big\|{A\operatorname{diag}^{1/2} \left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2$ conditioned on $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$.

 
 
\begin{lemma}\label{lem:M1-expec}
Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1 = \left\{{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\breve{P}\Gamma: A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\right\}$. There exist constants $\bar{c}_1,\bar{C}_2 > 1$, such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, conditional on $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$, it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$ that  
 
\[
\forall \ M_1\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1: \ 
\big\|{M_1\eta}\big\|_2^2 \in \left( \big\|{M_1}\big\|_F^2  - \bar{c}_1 \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right),\big\|{M_1}\big\|_F^2  + \bar{c}_1 \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right),
\]
 
where, 
 
\begin{align*}
\bar{F}_1&=\bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right), \;\text{and},\\
 
 
\bar{V}_1&=\bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{align*}
\end{lemma}
 
 
 
 
\begin{lemma}\label{lem:M2-expec}
Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2 = \left\{{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma: A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\right\}$. There exist constants $\bar{c}_1,\bar{C}_2 > 1$, such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, conditional on $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$, it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$ that 
 
\[
\forall \ M_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2: \ (1-p)\big\|{M_2\eta}\big\|_2^2 \le (1-p)\big\|{M_2}\big\|_F^2+\bar{c}_1(1-p) \left(\bar{F}_1 + \bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta) \right),
\]
 
 
where, 
 
\begin{align*}
\bar{F}_1&=\bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right), \; \text{and},\\
 
 
\bar{V}_1&=\bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{align*}
\end{lemma}
The proofs of Lemma~\ref{lem:M1-expec} and Lemma~\ref{lem:M2-expec} follow from an application of Theorem~\ref{thm:psd-sup} on appropriately defined sets of matrices. The complete proofs are available in Appendix~\ref{appsec:lem-M1-expec} and Appendix~\ref{appsec:lem-M2-expec} respectively.

For any $\delta \in (0,1)$, denote the events
 
 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{1a}} &\coloneq \left\{{\forall M_1 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1: \ \big\|{M_1\eta}\big\|_2^2 \in \left( \big\|{M_1}\big\|_F^2  - \bar{c}_1 \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right),\big\|{M_1}\big\|_F^2  + \bar{c}_1 \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right)}\right\}, \; \text{and,} \\
 
 
\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{2a}} &\coloneq \left\{{\forall M_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2: \ 
(1-p)\big\|{M_2\eta}\big\|_2^2 \le (1-p)\big\|{M_2}\big\|_F^2+\bar{c}_1(1-p) \left(\bar{F}_1 + \bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta) \right)
}\right\}.
\end{align*} 
Lemmas~\ref{lem:M1-expec} and ~\ref{lem:M2-expec} give us, 
 
 
$$\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{1a}} \vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T \right) \ge 1-\delta \text{ and } \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{2a}} \vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T\right) \ge 1-\delta.$$ 
 
 
Let $\bar{A} = \frac{\widehat{A}-B}{\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F} \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ and let $\bar{M}_1=(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A})\breve{P} \Gamma \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1$ and $\bar{M}_2 = \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2} (\bar{A}^\top \bar{A})\right)\breve{P}\Gamma \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2$. 
 
 
Then on $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{1a}} \cap \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{2a}}$ we have 
 
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\bar{M}_1\eta}\big\|_{2}^2 - (1-p)\big\|{\bar{M}_2\eta}\big\|_{2}^2 &\ge \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F^2- \bar{c}_1\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\\
&-(1-p)\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A})\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F^2
- (1-p)\bar{c}_1\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)
\end{align*}
and letting $\overline{P}=\breve{P}-pI$, we obtain
 
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\bar{M}_1\eta}\big\|_{2}^2 - (1-p)\big\|{\bar{M}_2\eta}\big\|_{2}^2\ge &\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\overline{P}\Gamma+p\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\Gamma}\big\|_F^2- c\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\\
&-(1-p)\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A})\right)\overline{P}\Gamma+p\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A})\right)\Gamma}\big\|_F^2,
\end{align*}
for some universal constant $c$. Expanding this gives us,
\begin{align}\label{eq:cond-bound}
&\big\|{\bar{M}_1\eta}\big\|_{2}^2 - (1-p)\big\|{\bar{M}_2\eta}\big\|_{2}^2  \nonumber \\
&\ge \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\overline{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F^2+ p^2 \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\Gamma}\big\|_F^2+2p\Big\langle\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\overline{P}\Gamma,\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\Gamma\Big\rangle  \nonumber\\
&-(1-p)\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\overline{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F^2-p^2(1-p)\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\Gamma}\big\|_F^2\nonumber\\
&-2p(1-p)\Big\langle\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\overline{P}\Gamma,\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\Gamma\Big\rangle-c \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right).
\end{align}

\@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@}{1.5ex plus
   0.5ex minus .2ex}{-1em}{\normalsize\bfseries}{Deriving bounds uniformly over all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$.} We now need to lower-bound the terms in the RHS of \eqref{eq:cond-bound}, uniformly over $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$. We start by defining a set of intermediate structures. Consider $\Gamma \Gamma^\top \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^{n(T-1) \times n(T-1)}$. Let $G_1,G_2,\dots,G_{T-1} \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^{n \times n}$ be a set of matrices where $G_t$ is the $t$-th block matrix on the diagonal of $\Gamma \Gamma^\top$, i.e.,
 
\[
G_t = \ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} 
(\Gamma \Gamma^\top)_{(t-1)n+1,(t-1)n+1} & (\Gamma \Gamma^\top)_{(t-1)n+1,(t-1)n+2} & \dots & (\Gamma \Gamma^\top)_{(t-1)n+1,tn}\\
(\Gamma \Gamma^\top)_{(t-1)n+2,(t-1)n+1} & (\Gamma \Gamma^\top)_{(t-1)n+2,(t-1)n+2} & \dots & (\Gamma \Gamma^\top)_{(t-1)n+2,tn}\\
\vdots & \vdots & \dots & \vdots\\
(\Gamma \Gamma^\top)_{tn,(t-1)n+1} & (\Gamma \Gamma^\top)_{tn,(t-1)n+2} & \dots & (\Gamma \Gamma^\top)_{tn,tn}\\
 \end{bmatrix}}.
\]
 
Let $G = \operatorname{blkdiag}(\ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix}  G_1 & G_2 & \dots & G_{T-1}  \end{bmatrix}})$. Also for all $t \in [T-1]$, let $q_t= \ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} \left(\overline{P}_t\right)_{11} \\ \left(\overline{P}_t\right)_{22} \\ \vdots \\ \left(\overline{P}_t\right)_{nn} \end{bmatrix}} \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^n$. Then for any $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$,
 
 
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\overline{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F^2 &= \Big\langle\overline{P}\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\overline{P},\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle\\
&=\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \Big\langleG_t,\overline{P}_t A^\top A \overline{P}_t\Big\rangle\\
&=\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} q_t^\top \left(G_t \odot A^\top A\right)q_t \\
& = q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right) q,
\end{align*}
 
where $q = \operatorname{vec}\left(\ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} q_1 & q_2 & \dots & q_{T-1} \end{bmatrix}}\right) \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^{n(T-1)}$. Note that since $G_t$ is positive semi-definite, by Schur Product Theorem, $\left(G_t \odot A^\top A\right)$ is positive semi-definite for all $t \in [T-1]$ and therefore $\left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right)$ is positive semi-definite. A similar argument shows that,
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\overline{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F^2 &= \Big\langle\overline{P}\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\overline{P},\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle\\
&=\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \Big\langleG_t,\overline{P}_t \operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right) \overline{P}_t\Big\rangle\\
&=\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} q_t^\top \left(G_t \odot \operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)q_t \\
& = q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right) q,
\end{align*}
 
where as before, $\left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right)$ is a positive semi-definite matrix. Furthermore, we have,
 
\begin{align*}
\Big\langle\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\overline{P}\Gamma,\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\Gamma\Big\rangle
&=\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle,
\end{align*}
 
and,
\begin{align*}
\Big\langle\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\overline{P}\Gamma,\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\Gamma\Big\rangle
&=\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle.
\end{align*}
 
Therefore,
\begin{align*}
&\big\|{AZ}\big\|_{F}^2 - (1-p)\big\|{A\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top)}\big\|_{F}^2 \\
&\ge q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right) q-(1-p)q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag} \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right) q\\
&+ p^2 \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\Gamma}\big\|_F^2-p^2(1-p)\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\Gamma}\big\|_F^2\\
&+2p\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A-(1-p)\operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle-c_1\bar{F}_1-\bar{V}_1\sqrt{U}.
\end{align*}

Now we need to uniformly bound,
 
\begin{enumerate}
\item $q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right) q$,
 
 
\item $(1-p)q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag} \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right) q$, and,
 
 
\item $2p\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A-(1-p)\operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle$,
\end{enumerate}
 
over all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$. We establish the following three lemmas which allows us to control each of the given three terms.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:M3-expec}
There exist  constants $\bar{c}_2,\bar{C}_2 > 1$, such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, with probability at least $1-\delta$, it holds for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ 
 
 
\[
q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)\right) q \in p(1-p) \left(\big\|{\left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)\right)^{1/2}}\big\|_F^2\pm \bar{c}_2\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right),
\]
 
where
 
\begin{equation*}
\bar{F}_1 = \bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left( \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right),
\bar{V}_1 = \bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}
 
 
 
 
\begin{lemma}\label{lem:M4-expec}
There exist  constants $\bar{c}_2,\bar{C}_2 > 1$, such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, with probability at least $1-\delta$, it holds for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ that
 
\[
q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}(A^\top A)\right)\right) q \le p(1-p) \left(\big\|{\left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}(A^\top A)\right)\right)^{1/2}}\big\|_F^2+\bar{c}_2\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right) 
\]
 
where
 
\begin{equation*}
\bar{F}_1 = \bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right),
 
 
\bar{V}_1 = \bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}
 
 
 
\begin{lemma}\label{lem:SR-expec} There exist constants $\widetilde{c}_1,\widetilde{c}_2 > 1$ such that for $\delta \in (0,1)$ the following statements hold.
 
\begin{enumerate}
\item With probability at least $1-\delta$, for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$,
 
\begin{equation*}
2p\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A-(1-p)\operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle 
\ge -\widetilde{c}_1pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right).
\end{equation*}
 


\item With probability at least $1-\delta$, for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$,
 
\[
2p\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle \le \widetilde{c}_2pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right),
\]

\item With probability at least  $1-\delta$, for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$,
 
\begin{equation*}
2p(1-p)\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag} \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle \le \widetilde{c}_2p(1-p)J(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right).
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

 
 
Proofs of Lemma~\ref{lem:M3-expec} (deferred to Appendix~\ref{appsec:lem-M3-expec}) and Lemma~\ref{lem:M4-expec} (deferred to Appendix~\ref{appsec:lem-M4-expec}) follow from applying Theorem~\ref{thm:psd-sup} on appropriately defined sets of matrices. The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:SR-expec} (deferred to Appendix~\ref{appsec:lem-SR-expec}) follows from applying  Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-func-conc}. 

Let us now denote for any $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$,
 
 
\begin{align*}
L_1 &= \left(\big\|{\left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)\right)^{1/2}}\big\|_F^2\pm \bar{c}_2\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right),\\ 
L_2 &=\left(\big\|{\left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}(A^\top A)\right)\right)^{1/2}}\big\|_F^2+\bar{c}_2\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right) \text{ and,}\\
L_3&=\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right), 
\end{align*}
 
 
and for any $\delta \in (0,1)$ the events
 
 
\begin{align*}
&\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{1b}} \coloneq \left\{{\forall  A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}: \ q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)\right) q \in p(1-p) L_1}\right\},\\
 
 
&\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{2b}} \coloneq \left\{{\forall  A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}: \ q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}(A^\top A)\right)\right) q \le p(1-p) L_2}\right\},\\
 
 
&\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{3b}} \coloneq \left\{{\forall  A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}: \ 2p\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A-(1-p)\operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle \ge -\widetilde{c}_1pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T}L_3}\right\}. 
\end{align*}
 
 
Here $\widetilde{c}_1, \bar{c}_2 > 1$ are constants. As an implication of Lemmas~\ref{lem:M3-expec}, ~\ref{lem:M4-expec}, and ~\ref{lem:SR-expec} we get $\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{1b}}\right) \ge 1-\delta$, $\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{2b}}\right) \ge 1-\delta$, and, $\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{3b}}\right) \ge 1-\delta$. 

\@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@}{1.5ex plus
   0.5ex minus .2ex}{-1em}{\normalsize\bfseries}{Putting it all together.} Consider the event $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{f}} = \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{1a}} \cap \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{2a}} \cap\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{1b}}\cap\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{2b}}\cap\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{3b}}$. We can see that under $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{E}}_{f}}$  with probability at least $1-5\delta$,
 
 
\begin{align*}
&\big\|{\bar{A} Z}\big\|_{F}^2 - (1-p)\big\|{\bar{A}\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top)}\big\|_{F}^2 \\
&\ge p(1-p)\operatorname{Tr} \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\right)+ p^2 \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\Gamma}\big\|_F^2\\
&-p(1-p)^2\operatorname{Tr} \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\right)- p^2(1-p) \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\Gamma}\big\|_F^2\\
&-\bar{c}_2 p(1-p)\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)-c_2pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\log (1/\delta)\right)-\bar{c}_1\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\\
&=p^2 \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \bar{A}\right)\Gamma}\big\|_F^2\\
&-\bar{c}_2 p(1-p)\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)-c_3 pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right)-\bar{c}_1\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\\
&\ge p^2 T-\bar{c}_2p(1-p)\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)-c_3 pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right)-\bar{c}_1\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\\
&\ge p^2 T -\bar{c}_1 J^2(A^*) \bar{L}_1- \bar{c}_2 p(1-p) J^2(A^*)\bar{L}_1 
-c_3 pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\log (1/\delta)\right)
\end{align*}
for universal constants $\bar{c}_1,\bar{c}_2,c_3,$ and, $\bar{C}$ where 
\begin{align*}
\bar{L}_1 &= \bar{C}^2 \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T} \log \left(1/\delta\right)\right).
\end{align*}
Multiplying both sides by $\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2$ and suitably rescaling $\delta$, we get the desired result.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- claim-1: body_md -->
\label{clm:quad-frob-norm}
Let $\breve{P} = \ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} P_1 & 0 & \dots & 0\\ 0 & P_2 & \dots & 0\\\vdots & \vdots & \dots & \vdots\\ 0 & 0 & \dots & P_{T-1} \end{bmatrix}}$, and $\bar{A} = \frac{\widehat{A}-B}{\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F}$. Then,
 
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{\left(\widehat{A}-B\right)Z}\big\|_F^2= \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2  \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \left(\breve{P} \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\breve{P}\right)\Gamma \eta,
\end{equation*}
and 
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{\left(\widehat{A}-B\right)\operatorname{diag}^{1/2} \left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2=\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2  \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \left(\breve{P} \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\right)\Gamma \eta.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end claim-1 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:M1-expec}
Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1 = \left\{{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\breve{P}\Gamma: A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\right\}$. There exist constants $\bar{c}_1,\bar{C}_2 > 1$, such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, conditional on $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$, it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$ that  
 
\[
\forall \ M_1\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1: \ 
\big\|{M_1\eta}\big\|_2^2 \in \left( \big\|{M_1}\big\|_F^2  - \bar{c}_1 \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right),\big\|{M_1}\big\|_F^2  + \bar{c}_1 \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right),
\]
 
where, 
 
\begin{align*}
\bar{F}_1&=\bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right), \;\text{and},\\
 
 
\bar{V}_1&=\bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:M2-expec}
Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2 = \left\{{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma: A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\right\}$. There exist constants $\bar{c}_1,\bar{C}_2 > 1$, such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, conditional on $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$, it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$ that 
 
\[
\forall \ M_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2: \ (1-p)\big\|{M_2\eta}\big\|_2^2 \le (1-p)\big\|{M_2}\big\|_F^2+\bar{c}_1(1-p) \left(\bar{F}_1 + \bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta) \right),
\]
 
 
where, 
 
\begin{align*}
\bar{F}_1&=\bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right), \; \text{and},\\
 
 
\bar{V}_1&=\bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:M3-expec}
There exist  constants $\bar{c}_2,\bar{C}_2 > 1$, such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, with probability at least $1-\delta$, it holds for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ 
 
 
\[
q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)\right) q \in p(1-p) \left(\big\|{\left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)\right)^{1/2}}\big\|_F^2\pm \bar{c}_2\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right),
\]
 
where
 
\begin{equation*}
\bar{F}_1 = \bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left( \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right),
\bar{V}_1 = \bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:M4-expec}
There exist  constants $\bar{c}_2,\bar{C}_2 > 1$, such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, with probability at least $1-\delta$, it holds for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ that
 
\[
q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}(A^\top A)\right)\right) q \le p(1-p) \left(\big\|{\left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}(A^\top A)\right)\right)^{1/2}}\big\|_F^2+\bar{c}_2\left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right) 
\]
 
where
 
\begin{equation*}
\bar{F}_1 = \bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right),
 
 
\bar{V}_1 = \bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:SR-expec} There exist constants $\widetilde{c}_1,\widetilde{c}_2 > 1$ such that for $\delta \in (0,1)$ the following statements hold.
 
\begin{enumerate}
\item With probability at least $1-\delta$, for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$,
 
\begin{equation*}
2p\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A-(1-p)\operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle 
\ge -\widetilde{c}_1pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right).
\end{equation*}
 


\item With probability at least $1-\delta$, for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$,
 
\[
2p\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle \le \widetilde{c}_2pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right),
\]

\item With probability at least  $1-\delta$, for all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$,
 
\begin{equation*}
2p(1-p)\Big\langle\overline{P},\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag} \left(A^\top A\right)\right)\Gamma \Gamma^\top\Big\rangle \le \widetilde{c}_2p(1-p)J(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right).
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
First consider $\big\|{\left(\widehat{A}-B\right)Z}\big\|_F^2$. 
 
Note that $\operatorname{vec}(Z) = \breve{P}x = \breve{P}\Gamma \eta$. Therefore,
 
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\left(\widehat{A}-B\right)Z}\big\|_F^2 &=\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2\big\|{\bar{A} Z}\big\|_F^2\\ 
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2\operatorname{Tr} \left(Z^\top \bar{A}^\top \bar{A} Z\right)\\
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2 \sum_{t=1}^{T-1} (Z_{:,t})^\top \bar{A}^\top \bar{A} Z_{:,t}\\
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2 \operatorname{vec}(Z)^\top \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\operatorname{vec}(Z)\\
&=\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2  \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \breve{P} \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma \eta.
\end{align*}
where $Z_{:,t}$ is the $t$-th column of $Z$.

Similarly for $\big\|{\left(\widehat{A}-B\right)\operatorname{diag}^{1/2} \left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2$, we get,
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\left(\widehat{A}-B\right)\operatorname{diag}^{1/2} \left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2 &=\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2\big\|{\bar{A}\operatorname{diag}^{1/2} \left(ZZ^\top\right)}\big\|_F^2\\ 
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2\Big\langle\bar{A}^\top \bar{A},\operatorname{diag}\left(ZZ^\top\right)\Big\rangle\\
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2\Big\langle\operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right),\operatorname{diag}\left(ZZ^\top\right)\Big\rangle\\
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2\sum_{i=1}^n \left(\operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)_{ii} \left({\operatorname{diag}\left(ZZ^\top\right)}\right)_{ii}\\
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2\sum_{i=1}^n \left(\operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)_{ii} \sum_{t=1}^{T-1} Z_{it}^2\\
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \sum_{i=1}^n \left(\operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right)_{ii}  Z_{it}^2\\
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \left(Z_{:,t}\right)^\top \operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right) Z_{:,t}\\
& = \big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2 \operatorname{vec}{(Z)}^\top \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right) \operatorname{vec}{(Z)}\\
&=\big\|{\widehat{A}-B}\big\|_F^2 \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \breve{P} \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(\bar{A}^\top \bar{A}\right)\right) \breve{P} \Gamma \eta.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:psd-sup} gives us
 
 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\sup_{M_1 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1} \ensuremath{\left | \big\|{M_1\eta}\big\|_2^2-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}\left[ \big\|{M_1\eta}\big\|_2^2 \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T \right] \right |} \ge c_1F_1+t \  \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T\right) \le 2\exp\left({-c_2 \min\left\{{\frac{t^2}{V_1^2}, \frac{t}{U_1}}\right\}}\right),
\end{align*}
 
where 
 
\begin{align*}
F_1 &= \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)\right)+d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1),\\
 
 
V_1 &= d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)\right), \text{ and } U_1 = d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)^2.
\end{align*}
 
Also for $M_1 = \left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\breve{P}\Gamma \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1$, we have
 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big\|{M_1\eta}\big\|_2^2 \ \big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T \right] &= \big\|{M_1}\big\|_F^2 \quad \text{(since entries of $\eta$ are of zero mean and unit variance.)} \\
& =\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F^2. 
\end{align*}
 
 
The terms $F_1,V_1$ and $U_1$ are dependent on $\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1$ instead of our tangent cone $\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$. In order to relate this to $\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ we first show that the mapping  $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)  \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)$ is Lipschitz. 
 
 
                                       
                                       
                                       
\begin{claim}\label{clm:lip-map}
The map $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right) \mapsto \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is $J(A^*)$-Lipschitz a.s.
\end{claim}
 
 
Since the map $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right) \mapsto \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is also onto, hence  Property~\ref{itm:prop-3}  from Appendix~\ref{sec:prelim-tools} implies,
 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \le \bar{C}_2 J(A^*) \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2),
\end{align*}
for a constant $\bar{C}_2 \ge 1$. Also, it holds a.s that
 
\begin{equation*}
d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1) = \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_2  \le \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{\breve{P}}\big\|_2\big\|{\Gamma}\big\|_2\big\|{A}\big\|_2 
 \le J(A^*),
\end{equation*}
 
and,
 
\begin{align*}
d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1) &= \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F \le \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\big\|{\breve{P}}\big\|_2\big\|{\Gamma}\big\|_2\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)}\big\|_F \\
&\le\sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} J(A^*) \sqrt{T} \big\|{A}\big\|_F \le J(A^*) \sqrt{T}.
\end{align*}
 

Let $t = V_1 \sqrt{u}$ for $u \ge 1$. Since $V_1 \ge U_1$ this means,
 
\[
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\sup_{M_1 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1} \ensuremath{\left | \big\|{M_1\eta}\big\|_2^2-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}\left[ \big\|{M_1 \eta}\big\|_2^2 \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T\right] \right |} \ge c_1F_1+V_1\sqrt{u} \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T \right) \le 2\exp (-c_2\sqrt{u}).
\]
 
Note that,
 
\begin{align*}
F_1 &= \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)\right)+d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)\\
&\le \bar{C}_2 J(A^*) \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \left(\bar{C}_2 J(A^*) \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+J(A^*) \sqrt{T}\right)+J^2(A^*) \sqrt{T}\\
&\le \bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right),
\end{align*}
 
and,
 
\begin{align*}
V_1 &= d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1)\right)\\
&\le J(A^*) \left(\bar{C}_2 J(A^*)\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +J(A^*)\sqrt{T}\right)\\
&\le \bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{align*}
 
Denoting the respective upper bounds by 
 
\begin{align}\label{eq:f1-v1-bar}
\bar{F}_1&=\bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right) \; \text{and},\\
 
 
\bar{V}_1&=\bar{C}_2 J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right),
\end{align}
 
we obtain,
 
\[
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\sup_{M_1 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1} \ensuremath{\left | \big\|{M_1\eta}\big\|_2^2-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big\|{M_1 \eta}\big\|_2^2 \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T \right] \right |} \ge c_1\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\sqrt{u} \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T \right) \le 2\exp (-c_2\sqrt{u}).
\]

Setting $u = c\log^2 (1/\delta)$ for a sufficiently large $c$ (such that $\sqrt{c}c_2 \ge 1$), we get that conditional on $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$, it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$, 
 
\begin{align*}
\forall M_1 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1: \ \big\|{M_1\eta}\big\|_2^2 \in \left( \big\|{M_1}\big\|_F^2  \pm \bar{c}_1 \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\right)
\end{align*}
where $\bar{c}_1$ depends on $c_1, c_2$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- claim-2: body_md -->
\label{clm:lip-map}
The map $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right) \mapsto \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is $J(A^*)$-Lipschitz a.s.
<!-- end claim-2 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Consider $A_1, A_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ and the corresponding terms $\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A_1\right)\breve{P}\Gamma \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1$ and, $\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A_2\right)\breve{P}\Gamma \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1$. Note that,
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A_1\right)\breve{P}\Gamma-\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A_2\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_2 & = \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(A_1-A_2\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_2\\
&\le \big\|{\breve{P}}\big\|_2 \big\|{\Gamma}\big\|_2 \big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_2\\
&\le J(A^*)\big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_2,
\end{align*}
 
and therefore, the map $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right) \mapsto \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is $J(A^*)$-Lipschitz.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:psd-sup} gives,
 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\sup_{M_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2} \ensuremath{\left | \big\|{M_2\eta}\big\|_2^2-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}}\left[ \big\|{M_2\eta}\big\|_2^2 \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T\right] \right |} \ge c_1F_2+t \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T \right) \le 2\exp\left({-c_2 \min\left\{{\frac{t^2}{V_2^2}, \frac{t}{U_2}}\right\}}\right),
\end{align*}
 
where
 
\begin{align*}
F_2 &= \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)\right)+d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2),\\ 
 
 
V_2 &= d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)\right), \text{ and } U_2 = d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)^2.
\end{align*}

For $M_2 = \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2$, note that
 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \left[\big\|{M_2\eta}\big\|_2^2 \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T \right] &= \big\|{M_2}\big\|_F^2 \quad \text{(Since entries of $\eta$ are of zero mean and unit variance.)} \\
& =\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F^2.
\end{align*}
 
The factors $F_2,V_2$ and $U_2$ depend on $\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2$ instead of  $\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$. To relate this to $\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ we proceed similarly as before.
 
 
                                       
                                       
                                       
\begin{claim}\label{clm:lip-map-M2}
The map $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right) \mapsto \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is $J(A^*)$-Lipschitz a.s.
\end{claim}
 
 
Since the map from $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right)$ to $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is also onto, hence Property~\ref{itm:prop-3} from Appendix~\ref{sec:prelim-tools} implies,
 
\[
\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \le \bar{C}_2 J(A^*) \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2),
\]
 
for a constant $\bar{C}_2 \ge 1$. Also, it holds a.s that
 
\begin{align*}
d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2) &= \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_2 \\
& \le \big\|{\breve{P}}\big\|_2\big\|{\Gamma}\big\|_2\sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\big\|{\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)}\big\|_2 \\
& \le J(A^*),
\end{align*}
 
and,
 
\begin{align*}
d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2) &= \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_F \\
& \le \big\|{\breve{P}}\big\|_2\big\|{\Gamma}\big\|_2\sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)\right)}\big\|_F \\
& \le J(A^*) \sqrt{T} \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A^\top A\right)}\big\|_F \\
&\le J(A^*) \sqrt{T}.
\end{align*}

Let $t = V_2 \sqrt{u}$ for $u \ge 1$. Since $V_2 \ge U_2$ this means,
 
\[
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\sup_{M_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2} \ensuremath{\left | \big\|{M_2\eta}\big\|_2^2-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \left[ \big\|{M_2 \eta}\big\|_2^2 \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T\right] \right |} \ge c_1F_2+V_2\sqrt{u} \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T\right) \le 2\exp (-c_2\sqrt{u}).
\]
 
Note that $F_2, V_2$ can be respectively bounded as
 
\begin{align*}
F_2 &= \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)\right)+d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)\\
&\le \bar{C}_2 J(A^*) \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \left(\bar{C}_2 J(A^*) \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+J(A^*) \sqrt{T}\right)+J^2(A^*) \sqrt{T}\\
&\le \bar{C}_2^2 J^2(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}^2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T}\right)\\
&=\bar{F}_1,
\end{align*}
 
and,
 
\begin{align*}
V_2 &= d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2)\right)\\
&\le J(A^*) \left(\bar{C}_2J(A^*)\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +J(A^*)\sqrt{T}\right)\\
&\le \bar{C}_2J^2(A^*)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) +\sqrt{T}\right)\\
&=\bar{V}_1.
\end{align*}
 
Then we obtain,
 
 
\[
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\sup_{M_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2} \ensuremath{\left | \big\|{M_2\eta}\big\|_2^2-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \left[ \big\|{M_2 \eta}\big\|_2^2 \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T\right] \right |} \ge c_1\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\sqrt{u} \Big\vert (P_t)_{t=1}^T\right) \le 2\exp (-c_2\sqrt{u}).
\]
 
 
Setting $u = c\log^2 (2/\delta)$ (for sufficiently large $c$) we get that, conditional on $(P_t)_{t=1}^T$, it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$, 
 
 
\begin{align*}
\forall M_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2: \ \big\|{M_2\eta}\big\|_2^2 \le \big\|{M_2}\big\|_F^2 + \bar{c}_1 \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right),
\end{align*}
 
 
where $\bar{c}_1$ depends on $c_1, c_2$.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- claim-3: body_md -->
\label{clm:lip-map-M2}
The map $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right) \mapsto \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is $J(A^*)$-Lipschitz a.s.
<!-- end claim-3 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Consider $A_1, A_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ and the corresponding $\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_1^\top A_1\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2$ and $\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_2^\top A_2\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_2$. Note that,
 
\begin{align*}
&\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_1^\top A_1\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma-\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_2^\top A_2\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_2\\ 
& = \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left(\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_1^\top A_1\right)-\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_2^\top A_2\right)\right)\right)\breve{P}\Gamma}\big\|_2\\
&\le \big\|{\breve{P}}\big\|_2 \big\|{\Gamma}\big\|_2 \big\|{\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_1^\top A_1\right)-\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_2^\top A_2\right)}\big\|_2\\
&\le J(A^*)\big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_2 \quad \text{a.s},
\end{align*}
 
and therefore, the map $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right) \mapsto \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_1,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is $J(A^*)$-Lipschitz a.s.

Note that the last inequality comes from the fact that  $\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_1^\top A_1\right)-\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_2^\top A_2\right)$ is diagonal and the $i$-th diagonal entry is $\big\|{(A_1)_{:,i}}\big\|_2-\big\|{(A_2)_{:,i}}\big\|_2$ (where $(A_1)_{:,i},(A_2)_{:,i}$ are the $i$-th columns of $A_1,A_2$),
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_1^\top A_1\right)-\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}\left(A_2^\top A_2\right)}\big\|_2 &\le \max_{i \in [n]} \ensuremath{\left | \big\|{(A_1)_{:,i}}\big\|_2-\big\|{(A_2)_{:,i}}\big\|_2 \right |}\\
&\le \max_{i \in [n]} \big\|{(A_1)_{:,i}-(A_2)_{:,i}}\big\|_2 \le \big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_2.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Given our goal of controlling $q^\top \left(G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)\right) q$ uniformly over all $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$, in order to apply Theorem~\ref{thm:psd-sup}, we need to first derive a matrix $\widetilde{M}_3$ such that $\widetilde{M}_3^\top \widetilde{M}_3 = G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)$. However, since our matrix is a Hadamard product, the definition of $\widetilde{M}_3$ is not directly apparent. We start by first deriving $\widetilde{M}_3$ such that $\widetilde{M}_3^\top \widetilde{M}_3 = G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)$. To do this we recall Theorem 1 of \cite{VISICK200045}. 

\begin{remark}[Theorem 1 of \cite{VISICK200045}]\label{rem:thm-had-kron}
Let $A,B \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^{n \times m}$. Then, 
\[
A \odot B = Q_n^\top \bigg(A \otimes B\bigg) Q_m
\]
where $Q_n^\top = \ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} \left(e_1^{(n)}{e_1^{(n)}}^\top\right) & \left(e_2^{(n)}{e_2^{(n)}}^\top\right) & \dots & \left(e_n^{(n)}{e_n^{(n)}}^\top\right) \end{bmatrix}}$ ($e_i^{(n)}$ is the $i$-th standard basis vector in $\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^n$).
\end{remark}
Using Remark~\ref{rem:thm-had-kron}, since $G$ is p.s.d, we can see that,
\begin{align*}
G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right) & = Q_{n(T-1)}^\top \left(G \otimes \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A^\top A)\right)\right) Q_{n(T-1)}\\
& = Q_{n(T-1)}^\top \left((G^{1/2} G^{1/2}) \otimes \left(\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A^\top\right)\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\right)\right) Q_{n(T-1)}\\
& = Q_{n(T-1)}^\top \left(\left(G^{1/2} \otimes \left(I_{T-1} \otimes A^\top\right)\right)\left(G^{1/2} \otimes \left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\right)\right) Q_{n(T-1)}\\
& = \widetilde{M}_3^\top \widetilde{M}_3 ,
\end{align*}
where $\widetilde{M}_3=\left(G^{1/2} \otimes \left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\right) Q_{n(T-1)}$.

Let $\widetilde{q} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{p(1-p)}}q$. It is easy to see that the variance of the entries of $\widetilde{q}$ is $1$. Let 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3 &= \left\{{\left(G^{1/2} \otimes \left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\right) Q_{n(T-1)}: A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\right\}. \end{align*}
 
 
Applying  Theorem~\ref{thm:psd-sup} gives us,
 
\[
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\sup_{M_3 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3} \ensuremath{\left | \big\|{M_3\widetilde{q}}\big\|_2^2-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \big\|{M_3\widetilde{q}}\big\|_2^2 \right |} \ge c_1F_3+t\right) \le 2\exp \left(-c_2 \min \left\{{\frac{t^2}{V_3},\frac{t}{U_3}}\right\}\right),
\]
 
where,
 
\begin{align*}
F_3 &= \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3)\right)+d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3)d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3),\\
 
 
V_3 &= d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3)\left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+d_{F}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3)\right), \; \text{and}\; U_3 = d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3)^2.
\end{align*}

Note that, the factors $F_3,V_3$ and $U_3$ are dependent on $\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3$ instead of  $\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$.  Proceeding as in the proof of Claim \ref{clm:lip-map-M2}, we have the following.
 
 
                                                                 
                                                                 
                                                                 
\begin{claim}\label{clm:lip-map-M3}
The map $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right) \mapsto \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is $J(A^*)$-Lipschitz.
\end{claim}
 
 
Since the map from $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2 \right)$ to $\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right)$ is Lipschitz and onto, Property \ref{itm:prop-3} from Appendix~\ref{sec:prelim-tools} implies,
 
\[
\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \le CJ(A^*) \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2),
\]
 
for some constant $C \ge 1$. Using the definitions of $d_2(\cdot), d_F(\cdot)$ from Section~\ref{sec:prelim-tools} gives us,
 
\begin{align*}
d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3) &= \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{\left(G^{1/2} \otimes \left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\right) Q_{n(T-1)}}\big\|_2 \\
&\le \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{\left(G^{1/2} \otimes \left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\right)}\big\|_2 \\
& \le \big\|{G}\big\|_2^{1/2} \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\big\|{A}\big\|_2 \\
& \le J(A^*),
\end{align*}
 
and,
 
\begin{align*}
d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}_3) &= \left(\sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{\left(G^{1/2} \otimes \left(I_{T-1} \otimes A\right)\right) Q_{n(T-1)}}\big\|_F^2\right)^{1/2} \\
