\begin{claim}\label{clm:lip-map-calS}
The following statements hold.
 
 
\begin{enumerate}
\item The map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)$ is $(3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2$-Lipschitz, and,
\item The map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$ is $(3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2\sqrt{T}$-Lipschitz.
\end{enumerate}
\end{claim}

Using Property~\ref{itm:prop-3} from Appendix~\ref{sec:prelim-tools}, we get,
\begin{align*}
&\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right) \le \bar{c}_1 (3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right),\\
&\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right) \le \bar{c}_2 (3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2\sqrt{T} \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right).
\end{align*}
We can also see that,
\begin{align*}
d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}) &= \sup_{S\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}} \big\|{S}\big\|_2 
= \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{S_1(A)+S_2(A)+S_3(A)}\big\|_2\\
& \le (3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2  \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{A}\big\|_2  \\
& \le (3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2, \\
 
 
\text{ and } \ d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}) &= \sup_{S\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}} \big\|{S}\big\|_F = \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{S_1(A)+S_2(A)+S_3(A)}\big\|_F\\
& \le (3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2\sqrt{T} \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}   \big\|{A}\big\|_F \\
& = (3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2\sqrt{T}.
\end{align*}

Therefore, with probability at least $1-e^{-u}$,
\begin{align*}
&\sup_{S \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}} \ensuremath{\left | \bar{q}^\top S \bar{q}-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \bar{q}^\top S \bar{q} \right |} \\
&\le (3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \bigg(\bar{c}_1 \ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+\bar{c}_2 \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{u} \sqrt{T}+u\bigg).
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- claim-11: body_md -->
\label{clm:lip-map-calS}
The following statements hold.
 
 
\begin{enumerate}
\item The map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)$ is $(3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2$-Lipschitz, and,
\item The map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$ is $(3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2\sqrt{T}$-Lipschitz.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end claim-11 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Let $A_1,A_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$, and $\widetilde{S}_1,\widetilde{S}_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}$ be such that $\widetilde{S}_1 = S_1(A_1)+S_2(A_1)+S_3(A_1)$ and $\widetilde{S}_2 = S_1(A_2)+S_2(A_2)+S_3(A_2)$. We will start with the map, $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)$. Note that,
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\widetilde{S}_1-\widetilde{S}_2}\big\|_2 &= \big\|{S_1(A_1)+S_2(A_1)+S_3(A_1)-S_1(A_2)-S_2(A_2)-S_3(A_2)}\big\|_2\\
&\le \big\|{S_1(A_1)-S_1(A_2)}\big\|_2+\big\|{S_2(A_1)-S_2(A_2)}\big\|_2+\big\|{S_3(A_1)-S_3(A_2)}\big\|_2\\
&=\big\|{\left(\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A^*\right)G\right) \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A_1-A_2)\right)}\big\|_2\\
&+(1-p)\big\|{G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left((A_1-A_2)^\top A^*\right)\right)}\big\|_2+\big\|{G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left({A^*}^\top (A_1-A_2)\right)\right)}\big\|_2\\
& \le \big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A^*\right)G}\big\|_2\big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_2+(1-p)\big\|{G}\big\|_2\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2\big\|{(A_1-A_2}\big\|_2+\big\|{G}\big\|_2 \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2\big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_2\\
&\le (3-p)J^2(A^*) \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \big\|{(A_1-A_2}\big\|_2.
\end{align*}
Therefore, the map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)$ is $(3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2$-Lipschitz.

Now consider the map, $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$. We can see that,
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\widetilde{S}_1-\widetilde{S}_2}\big\|_F &= \big\|{S_1(A_1)+S_2(A_1)+S_3(A_1)-S_1(A_2)-S_2(A_2)-S_3(A_2)}\big\|_F\\
&\le \big\|{S_1(A_1)-S_1(A_2)}\big\|_F+\big\|{S_2(A_1)-S_2(A_2)}\big\|_F+\big\|{S_3(A_1)-S_3(A_2)}\big\|_F\\
&=\big\|{\left(\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A^*\right)G\right) \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes (A_1-A_2)\right)}\big\|_F\\
&+(1-p)\big\|{G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left((A_1-A_2)^\top A^*\right)\right)}\big\|_F+\big\|{G \odot \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left({A^*}^\top (A_1-A_2)\right)\right)}\big\|_F\\
& \le \left(\big\|{\left(I_{T-1} \otimes A^*\right)G}\big\|_2+(1-p)\big\|{G}\big\|_2\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2+\big\|{G}\big\|_2 \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2\right)\sqrt{T}\big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_F\\
&\le (3-p)J^2(A^*) \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T}\big\|{(A_1-A_2}\big\|_F.
\end{align*}
Therefore, the map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$ is $(3-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2\sqrt{T}$-Lipschitz.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Consider 
\begin{align*}
f_2(A) &= \bigg | p\operatorname{Tr} \left( \Gamma^\top \left((I_{T-1} \otimes {A^*}^\top) \overline{P}_2 (I_{T-1} \otimes A) \right) \Gamma\right)+2p(1-p)\operatorname{Tr} \left(\Gamma^\top \overline{P}_1 \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A^*\right)\right)  \Gamma\right)\\
&- p\operatorname{Tr} \left( \Gamma^\top \left(\overline{P}_1 \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left({A^*}^\top A\right)\right)  \right) \Gamma\right) \bigg |.
\end{align*}
Note that, using the properties of trace, we can rearrange the terms to get,
\begin{align*}
f_2(A) &= \bigg | p\Big\langle(I_{T-1} \otimes {A^*}) \Gamma \Gamma^\top (I_{T-1} \otimes {A}^\top),\overline{P}_2\Big\rangle+2p(1-p)\Big\langle\Gamma \Gamma^\top \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left({A^*}^\top A\right)\right) ,\overline{P}_1\Big\rangle\\
&- p\Big\langle\Gamma \Gamma^\top \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left({A}^\top A^*\right)\right),\overline{P}_1\Big\rangle \bigg |.
\end{align*}
For any $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$, let,
\begin{align*}
H(A) &= p\ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} 0_{n \times n} & 0_{n \times n(T-1)} \\ 0_{n(T-1) \times n} & (I_{T-1} \otimes {A^*}) \Gamma \Gamma^\top (I_{T-1} \otimes {A}^\top) \end{bmatrix}}-p\ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix}  \Gamma \Gamma^\top \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left({A}^\top A^*\right)\right) & 0_{n(T-1) \times n}\\0_{n \times n(T-1)} & 0_{n \times n} \end{bmatrix}}\\
&+2p(1-p)\ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} \Gamma \Gamma^\top \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left({A^*}^\top A\right)\right)  & 0_{n(T-1) \times n}\\ 0_{n \times n(T-1)} & 0_{n \times n} \end{bmatrix}}.
\end{align*}
Note that then for any $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$, we have $f_2(A) = \Big\langleH(A),\overline{P}\Big\rangle$ where 
 
 
$$\overline{P} = \ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} P_1 & 0 & 0 & \dots & 0 \\ 0 & P_2 & 0 & \dots & 0 \\ \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \dots & \vdots \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & \dots & P_T \end{bmatrix}}-pI.$$ 
 
Since $\overline{P}$ is a diagonal matrix, the only entries that affect the trace are the diagonal entries of $H(A)$. Following a simple calculation, we can show that the matrix consisting of the diagonal entries of $H(A)$ (denoted by $H_D(A)$) can be written as,
\begin{align*}
& H_D(A) \\
&= p\ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} 
0 & 0 & \dots & 0 \\ 
0 & \operatorname{diag}\left(A^* G_1 A^\top\right) & \dots & 0\\ 
\vdots & \vdots & \dots & \vdots\\
0 & 0 & \dots & \operatorname{diag}\left(A^* G_{T-1} A^\top\right)
 \end{bmatrix}}\\
&+2p(1-p)\ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} \operatorname{diag}\left(G_1\operatorname{diag}\left( {A^{*}}^\top A\right)\right) & 0 & \dots & 0 & 0 \\
0 & \operatorname{diag}\left(G_2\operatorname{diag}\left( {A^{*}}^\top A\right)\right) & \dots & 0 & 0\\
\vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots & \vdots \\
0 & 0 & \dots & \operatorname{diag}\left(G_{T-1}\operatorname{diag}\left( {A^{*}}^\top A\right)\right) & 0\\
0 & 0 & \dots & 0 & 0 \end{bmatrix}}\\
&-p\ensuremath{\begin{bmatrix} \operatorname{diag}\left(G_1 A^\top A^*\right) & 0 & \dots & 0 & 0 \\
0 & \operatorname{diag}\left(G_2 A^\top A^*\right) & \dots & 0 & 0\\
\vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots & \vdots \\
0 & 0 & \dots & \operatorname{diag}\left(G_{T-1} A^\top A^*\right) & 0\\
0 & 0 & \dots & 0 & 0 \end{bmatrix}},
\end{align*}
 
 
and $\Big\langleH(A),\overline{P}\Big\rangle = \Big\langleH_D(A),\overline{P}\Big\rangle$. Let $h(A)$ be a vector such that $h(A)_i = H_D(A)_{ii}$. Then, we can also see that, $\Big\langleH(A),\overline{P}\Big\rangle =\Big\langleh(A),\bar{q}\Big\rangle$. Now let,
\begin{equation*}
\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}} = \left\{{h(A): \ A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\right\}, \quad \text{and} \quad
\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat} = \left\{{H_D(A): \ A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\right\}.
\end{equation*}
Note that by definition, the map, $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$ is a one-to-one and onto mapping. Therefore, we can see that $\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)=\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$. Also note that since each $q_i \in [-p,1-p]$ and centered, we have a vector $q$ with zero mean, 1-subgaussian entries. Applying the Remark~\ref{rem:gaus-width} on $\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}$ implies $\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \sup_{h \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}} \Big\langleq,h\Big\rangle \le c w(\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}})$ and from Talagrand's majorizing measure theorem~\cite[Theorem 2.4.1]{talagrand2014upper}, 
 
\[
w(\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}) \asymp \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2\right) = \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right).
\]
Furthermore, letting $g(x) = \sup_{h \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}} \Big\langlex,h\Big\rangle$, we can see that, 
\begin{align*}
&\ensuremath{\left | g(x_1)-g(x_2) \right |} 
= \ensuremath{\left | \sup_{h \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}} \Big\langlex_1,h\Big\rangle-\sup_{h \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}} \Big\langlex_2,h\Big\rangle \right |} \\
& \le \sup_{h \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}} \big\|{h}\big\|_2 \big\|{x_1-x_2}\big\|_2\\
& \le \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{h(A)}\big\|_2 \big\|{x_1-x_2}\big\|_2\\
& \le \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \bigg(p\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \big\|{\operatorname{diag}(A^*G_tA^\top)}\big\|_F^2\right)^{1/2}+2p(1-p)\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \big\|{\operatorname{diag}(G_t\operatorname{diag}({A^*}^\top A)}\big\|_F^2\right)^{1/2}\\
&+p\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \big\|{\operatorname{diag}(G_tA^\top A^*)}\big\|_F^2\right)^{1/2}\bigg) \big\|{x_1-x_2}\big\|_2\\
& \le \sup_{A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}  \bigg(p \big\|{G}\big\|_2 \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \big\|{A}\big\|_F+2p(1-p)\big\|{G}\big\|_2 \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \big\|{A}\big\|_F\\
& +p\big\|{G}\big\|_2 \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \big\|{A}\big\|_F\bigg) \big\|{x_1-x_2}\big\|_2\\
&\le 2p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \big\|{x_1-x_2}\big\|_2.
\end{align*}
Therefore, $g_1(x)$ is $2p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T}$-Lipschitz with respect to $\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2$. Therefore, applying the Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-func-conc} on $g(q)$ gives us, for any $t>0$,
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\ensuremath{\left | \sup_{h \in H} \Big\langleq,h\Big\rangle-\ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \sup_{h \in H} \Big\langleq,h\Big\rangle \right |} \ge t\right) \le 2\exp\left(-\frac{t^2}{8p^2(2-p)^2J^4(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2^2 T}\right).
\end{align*}
Letting $t = c 2p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}$ (for $\delta>0$), we have, 
\begin{align*}
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \left(\sup_{h \in H} \Big\langleq,h\Big\rangle \ge \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \sup_{h \in H} \Big\langleq,h\Big\rangle+ c 2p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right) \le 2\exp\left(-c\log (1/\delta)\right).
\end{align*}
and therefore, with probability at least $1-\delta$, 
\begin{align*}
\sup_{h \in H} \Big\langleq,h\Big\rangle &\le \ensuremath{\mathbb{E}} \sup_{h \in H} \Big\langleq,h\Big\rangle+ c p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\\ 
&\le c\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)+ c p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}.
\end{align*}
Now consider the map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$. We can see that this map is onto by definitions of $\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}$ and $\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat}$. We next show that this map is Lipschitz as well.
\begin{claim}\label{clm:lip-map-calH}
The map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$ is $2p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T}$-Lipschitz.
\end{claim}
Using Claim~\ref{clm:lip-map-calH}, and the Property~\ref{itm:prop-3} from Appendix~\ref{sec:prelim-tools}, we can see that, 
\[
\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \le \bar{c}_1 p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)
\]
and therefore, with probability at least $1-\delta$,
\begin{align*}
\sup_{h \in H} \Big\langleq,h\Big\rangle &\le \widetilde{c}p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \left( \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right).
\end{align*}
which implies with probability at least $1-\delta$, for any $A \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$,
\begin{align*}
&\bigg | p\operatorname{Tr} \left( \Gamma^\top \left((I_{T-1} \otimes {A^*}^\top) \overline{P}_2 (I_{T-1} \otimes A) \right) \Gamma\right)+2p(1-p)\operatorname{Tr} \left(\Gamma^\top \overline{P}_1 \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}\left(A^\top A^*\right)\right)  \Gamma\right)\\
&- p\operatorname{Tr} \left( \Gamma^\top \left(\overline{P}_1 \left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left({A^*}^\top A\right)\right)  \right) \Gamma\right) \bigg |\\
& \le \widetilde{c}p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \left( \ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right).
\end{align*}
for some constant $\widetilde{c}$.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- claim-12: body_md -->
\label{clm:lip-map-calH}
The map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$ is $2p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T}$-Lipschitz.
<!-- end claim-12 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Let $A_1, A_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$ and let $H_1, H_2$, such that $H_1 = H_D(A_1)$ and $H_2=H_D(A_2)$. Then,
\begin{align*}
&\big\|{H_1-H_2}\big\|_F \\
&= \big\|{H_D(A_1)-H_D(A_2)}\big\|_F\\
&=\big\|{H_D(A_1-A_2)}\big\|_F \; \text{(since $H_D(A)$ is linear)}\\
& \le  p\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \big\|{\operatorname{diag}(A^*G_t(A_1-A_2)^\top)}\big\|_F^2\right)^{1/2}+2p(1-p)\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \big\|{\operatorname{diag}(G_t\operatorname{diag}({A^*}^\top (A_1-A_2))}\big\|_F^2\right)^{1/2}\\
&+p\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \big\|{\operatorname{diag}(G_t(A_1-A_2)^\top A^*)}\big\|_F^2\right)^{1/2}\\
& \le  p \big\|{G}\big\|_2 \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_F+2p(1-p)\big\|{G}\big\|_2 \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_F+p\big\|{G}\big\|_2 \big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_F\\
&\le 2p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T} \big\|{A_1-A_2}\big\|_F.
\end{align*}
Therefore, the map $(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) \mapsto (\ensuremath{\mathcal{H}}_{mat}, \big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)$ is $2p(2-p)J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2 \sqrt{T}$-Lipschitz.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:PGD-error-decomp}
    For $p \in (0,1]$, consider the objective function
    \begin{equation*}
        g(A) = \big\|{\widetilde Z - AZ}\big\|_F^2 - (1-p) \big\|{A\operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top)}\big\|_F^2.
    \end{equation*}
 



Recall the projected gradient descent (PGD) algorithm
\begin{equation*}
    A^{(k+1)} = \Pi_{\cal K}(A^{(k)} - \gamma \nabla g(A^{(k)})), 
\end{equation*}
where $A^{(k)}$ is the $k$-th iteration of the algorithm computed from the data, $\gamma > 0$ is the learning rate, and $\Pi_{\cal K}(.)$ is the orthogonal projection onto the set $\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$. Recall that 
 
\begin{align*}
    D = \operatorname{diag}^{1/2}(ZZ^\top), \quad M = ZZ^\top - (1-p)D, \quad \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B} = \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}, B} \cap \mathbb{S}_n,
\end{align*}
 
 and denote the normalized directions as
\begin{equation*}
    H^{(k)}_B := \frac{A^{(k)} - B}{\big\|{A^{(k)}-B}\big\|_F}, \quad H^*_B := \frac{A^* - B}{\big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F}.
\end{equation*}
It holds that 
\begin{align*}
    &\big\|{A^{(k+1)} -B}\big\|_F \leq \big\|{A^{(k)} - B}\big\|_F \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B } \Big\langleV,H_B^{(k)}(I - 2\gamma \mathbb{E}[M]\Big\rangle 
    \\&+ 2\gamma\big\|{A^{(k)} - B}\big\|_F \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B } \Big\langleV,H_B^{(k)} (\mathbb{E}[M] - M)\Big\rangle  
    \\&+ 2\gamma \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \left\{ \big\|{VZ}\big\|_F \big\|{(A^* - B) Z}\big\|_F + (1-p)\big\|{VD}\big\|_F \big\|{(A^* - B)D}\big\|_F 
    + \Big\langleVZ,\widetilde Z - A^* Z\Big\rangle + (1-p)\Big\langleVD,A^* D\Big\rangle \right\} \\
    &=: (I) + (II) + (III).
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
By Lemma \ref{lem:proj-to-cone}, we have
\begin{align*}
    \big\|{A^{(k+1)} - B}\big\|_F &= \big\|{\Pi_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}}\left(A^{(k)} - \gamma \nabla g(A^{(k)}) \right) - B}\big\|_F \\
    &\leq \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B}\Big\langleV,A^{(k)} - \gamma \nabla g(A^{(k)}) - B\Big\rangle \\
    &= \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \Big\langleV,A^{(k)} - B - \gamma\left[\nabla g(A^{(k)}) - \nabla g(B) \right] - \gamma\left[\nabla g(B) - \nabla g(A^*) \right] - \gamma \nabla g(A^*)\Big\rangle.
\end{align*}
The gradients above can be calculated explicitly as $\nabla g(A^{(k)}) - \nabla g(B) = 2(A^{(k)} - B)M$, $\nabla g(B) - \nabla g(A^*) = 2(B - A^*)M$, and $\nabla g(A^*) = 2[A^*M - \widetilde Z Z^\top]$. Substituting into the last display, we obtain
\begin{align*}
    \big\|{A^{(k+1)} - B}\big\|_F &\leq \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \left\{ \Big\langleV,(A^{(k)} - B)(I - 2\gamma M\Big\rangle + 2\gamma \Big\langleV,(A^* - B)M\Big\rangle - 2\gamma\Big\langleV,A^*M - \widetilde Z Z^\top\Big\rangle \right\}
\end{align*}
Intercalating by $\mathbb{E}[M]$, we obtain 
\begin{align*}
    &\big\|{A^{(k+1)} - B}\big\|_F \leq \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \Big\langleV,(A^{(k)} - B)(I - 2\gamma \mathbb{E}[M])\Big\rangle + 2\gamma \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \Big\langleV,(A^{(k)} - B)(\mathbb{E}[M] - M)\Big\rangle \\
    &+ 2\gamma \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \left\{ \Big\langleV,(A^* - B)Z Z^\top\Big\rangle - (1-p)\Big\langleV,(A^* - B)DD^\top\Big\rangle + \Big\langleV,(\widetilde Z - A^* Z)Z^\top + (1-p)A^* DD^\top\Big\rangle \right\}.
\end{align*}
Normalizing by $\big\|{A^{(k)} - B}\big\|_F$, and using the cyclic properties of the trace, we have
\begin{align*}
    &\big\|{A^{(k+1)} - B}\big\|_F \leq \big\|{A^{(k)} - B}\big\|_F \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \Big\langleV,H_B^{(k)}(I_n - 2\gamma \mathbb{E}[M])\Big\rangle \\
    &+ 2\gamma \big\|{A^{(k)} - B}\big\|_F\sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \Big\langleV,H_B^{(k)}(\mathbb{E}[M] - M)\Big\rangle \\
    &+ 2\gamma \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \left\{ \Big\langleVZ,(A^* - B)Z\Big\rangle - (1-p)\Big\langleVD,(A^* - B)D\Big\rangle + \Big\langleVZ,\widetilde Z - A^* Z\Big\rangle + (1-p)\Big\langleVD,A^* D\Big\rangle \right\} \\
    &=:(I) + (II) + (IIA). 
\end{align*}
For $(IIA)$, we have by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, 
\begin{align*}
    (IIA) &\leq 2\gamma \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} &\left\{ \big\|{VZ}\big\|_F \big\|{(A^* - B) Z}\big\|_F + (1-p)\big\|{VD}\big\|_F \big\|{(A^* - B)D}\big\|_F 
    + \Big\langleVZ,\widetilde Z - A^* Z\Big\rangle + (1-p)\Big\langleVD,A^* D\Big\rangle \right\} \\
    &=: (III),
\end{align*}
and putting together $(I), (II)$ and $(III)$ in the last display completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:term-1-PGD-decomp}
    Suppose that the learning rate is chosen as 
    \begin{equation*}
        \gamma = \frac{1}{2p^2 T J^2(A^*)}.
    \end{equation*}
Then, it holds for all $k = 0,1,2,\dots$ that
    \begin{equation*}
        \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \Big\langleV,H_B^{(k)}(I - 2\gamma \mathbb{E}[M]\Big\rangle_F  \leq \left(1 - \frac{1}{2J^2(A^*)}\right).
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
We first bound the maximum and minimum eigenvalues of $\mathbb{E}[M]$, and use it to bound $(I)$. 

In Lemma \ref{lem:unb-est}, the conditional expectation of $M$ given $(P_1, \dots, P_T)$ was derived. By the tower property, we have 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:expec-M-pgd}
    \mathbb{E}[M] = \mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[M \mid \mathcal (P_t)_{t=1}^T)]] = \mathbb{E}[p^2 XX^\top] = p^2 \mathbb{E}[X X^\top]. 
\end{equation}
Denote $\eta = \operatorname{vec}([\eta_1, \dots, \eta_{T-1}])$ to be formed by column-wise vectorization, and $$\Gamma_t = [(A^*)^{t-1}, (A^*)^{t-2}, \dots, I, 0, \dots 0] \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times n(T-1)}.$$ Moreover, let $\Gamma = [\Gamma_1, \dots, \Gamma_{T-1}^\top$], so that $X_t = \Gamma_t \eta$. Since the entries of $\eta_t$ are i.i.d centered random variables with unit variance, we have by expanding the outer product on the RHS of \eqref{eq:expec-M-pgd}, 
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}[M] &= p^2 \mathbb{E}[XX^\top] = p^2 \sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\Gamma_t \mathbb{E}[\eta \eta^\top]\Gamma_t^\top = p^2 \sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \Gamma_t \Gamma_t^\top.
\end{align*}
By expanding the outer product involving $\Gamma_t$, it is easy to see that $\Gamma_t \Gamma_t \succeq I$. Then from the last display, we have by the symmetry, as soon as $T \geq 2$
of $\mathbb{E}[M]$
\begin{equation} \label{eq:pgd_contrac_lambmin}
    \mathbb{E}[M] \succeq p^2(T-1)I_n \implies \lambda_{\min}(\mathbb{E}[M]) \geq p^2(T-1) \geq p^2T/2. 
\end{equation}
Moreover, by Weyl's inequality for the sum of symmetric matrices, we have 
\begin{align}
    \lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[M]) &\leq p^2 \sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\lambda_{\max}(\Gamma_t \Gamma_t^\top) = p^2 \sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \|\Gamma_t\|_2^2 \nonumber \\
    &\leq p^2 (T-1)\|\Gamma\|_2^2 \nonumber  \leq p^2 T J^2(A^*). \label{eq:lambda-max-EM}
\end{align}
 
 
We now proceed to bound $(I)$. Set the learning rate to $\gamma = (2p^2 T J^2(A^*))^{-1}$, and fix $V \in \mathbb{S}_n$. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,  the submultiplicativity of matrix norms, and \eqref{eq:pgd_contrac_lambmin}, 
we have for our choice of $\gamma$ that
 
\begin{align*}
    \Big\langleV,H^{(k)}_B (I_n - 2\gamma \mathbb{E}[M])\Big\rangle &\leq  \big\|{V}\big\|_F\big\|{H^{(k)}_B(I_n - 2\gamma \mathbb{E}[M])}\big\|_F \\
    &\leq  \big\|{H^{(k)}_B}\big\|_F \big\|{I_n - 2\gamma \mathbb{E}[M]}\big\|_2 \\
    &=  \big\|{I_n - 2\gamma \mathbb{E}[M]}\big\|_2 \\
    &\leq 1 - 2\gamma \lambda_{min}(\mathbb{E}[M])  = 1 - \frac{1}{2J^2(A^*)} , \tag{using \eqref{eq:pgd_contrac_lambmin}} 
\end{align*}
 
which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:PGD-centered-term}
   Suppose the learning rate is chosen as 
       \begin{equation*}
        \gamma = \frac{1}{2p^2 T J^2(A^*) }.
    \end{equation*}
    Then for $\delta \in (0, 1)$, it holds with probability at least $1 - \delta$ for all $k = 0,1,2,\dots$ that
     
   \begin{equation*}
   2\gamma \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \Big\langleV,H_B^{(k)}(\mathbb{E}[M] - M)\Big\rangle \leq
    \frac{C}{p^2}\left(\frac{\gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_2)}{T} + \frac{\gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_F)}{\sqrt{T}} + \frac{\log(1/\delta)}{T} + \frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{T}} \right).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
Since $A^{(k)}$ and $B$ lies in the tangent cone, we have $H_B^{(k)} \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}$. Thus,  
\begin{align*}
    \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \Big\langleV,H_B^{(k)}(\mathbb{E}[M] - M)\Big\rangle \leq \sup_{U,V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \Big\langleU^\top V,\mathbb{E}[M] - M\Big\rangle,
\end{align*}
where we recall that $M = ZZ^\top - (1-p)DD^\top$. We will rewrite the RHS of the last display as a suitable quadratic form, before applying Theorem \ref{thm:non-psd-sup}.

To this end, note that in the proof of Claim \ref{clm:quad-frob-norm} in Appendix \ref{appsec:clm-quad-frob-norm}, we have shown that 
\begin{equation*}
    \Big\langleA^\top A,\operatorname{diag}(ZZ^\top)\Big\rangle = \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \check P (I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}(A^\top A))\check P \Gamma \eta.
\end{equation*}
In fact, the identity above holds for any $U, V \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times n}$ in place of $A^\top$ and $A$, noting that all the terms other than $A$ on the RHS in the last display do not depend on $A$. Thus, we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \Big\langleU^\top V,DD^\top\Big\rangle = \Big\langleU^\top V,\operatorname{diag}(ZZ^\top)\Big\rangle = \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \check P (I_{T-1} \otimes \operatorname{diag}(U^\top V))\check P \Gamma \eta 
\end{equation*}
Moreover, we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \Big\langleU^\top V,Z Z^\top\Big\rangle = \operatorname{Tr}(Z^\top V^\top U Z) = \operatorname{vec}(Z^\top)\left(I_{T-1} \otimes U^\top V \right)\operatorname{vec}(Z
) = \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \check P \left(I_{T-1} \otimes U^\top V \right)\check P \Gamma \eta.
\end{equation*}
Thus, we have
\begin{align*}
    \Big\langleU^\top V,M\Big\rangle &= \Big\langleU^\top V,ZZ^\top - (1-p)DD^\top\Big\rangle = \eta^\top \Gamma^\top \check P\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left\{U^\top V - (1-p)\operatorname{diag}(U^\top V) \right\} \right)P\Gamma \eta. 
\end{align*}
Define the matrix valued-map 
\begin{equation*}
    R:\begin{cases}
        \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B \times \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B \mapsto \mathbb{R}^{n(T-1)\times n(T-1)}, \\
        (U,V) \mapsto R_{U,V},
    \end{cases}
\end{equation*}
where
\begin{equation*}
    R_{U,V} = \Gamma^\top \check P\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left\{U^\top V - (1-p)\operatorname{diag}(U^\top V) \right\} \right)P\Gamma,
\end{equation*}
and is surjective onto $\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}} := \left\{R_{U,V}: U, V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B \right\}$ by construction. Then applying Theorem \ref{thm:non-psd-sup}, we have with probability at least $1 - \delta$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quad-form-bound-PGD}
    \sup_{w \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}} \left|\eta^\top w\eta - \mathbb{E}[\eta^\top w\eta] \right| \leq C \left(\gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}, \|.\|_2 + \gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}, \|.\|_F) + \sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}) + \log(1/\delta)d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}) \right),
\end{equation}
where $C > 0$ is some absolute constant. 
Note that although $\eta$ is $L$-subgaussian, a rescaling allows one to obtain a 1-subgaussian random vector, which only affects the constants in the resulting bound above.

We now proceed to bound the terms on the RHS of \eqref{eq:quad-form-bound-PGD}. By the triangle inequality, and repeated applications of the sub-multiplicativity of norms, we have 
\begin{align*}
    d_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}) &= \sup_{w \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}} \big\|{w}\big\|_2 = \sup_{U,V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \big\|{\Gamma^\top \check P\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left\{U^\top V - (1-p)\operatorname{diag}(U^\top V) \right\} \right)P\Gamma}\big\|_2 \\
    &\leq \sup_{U,V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \big\|{\Gamma}\big\|_2^2 \big\|{\check P}\big\|\|_2^2 \big\|{U^\top V - (1-p)\operatorname{diag}(U^\top V)}\big\|_2  \\
    &\leq J^2(A^*)  \sup_{U,V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \left(\big\|{U^\top V}\big\|_2 + (1-p)\big\|{\operatorname{diag}(U^\top V)}\big\|_2 \right) \\
    &\leq J^2(A^*)(2-p)\sup_{U,V\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \big\|{U^\top V}\big\|_2 \leq 2J^2(A^*).
\end{align*}

On the other hand, by using the similar lines of arguments as above, we have
\begin{align*}
    d_F(\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}) &= \sup_{w \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}} \big\|{w}\big\|_F = \sup_{U,V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \big\|{\Gamma^\top \check P\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left\{U^\top V - (1-p)\operatorname{diag}(U^\top V) \right\} \right)P\Gamma}\big\|_F \\
    &\leq \sup_{U,V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \big\|{\Gamma}\big\|_2^2 \big\|{\check P}\big\|_2^2 \sqrt{T-1}\left(\big\|{U^\top V}\big\|_F + (1-p) \big\|{\operatorname{diag}(U^\top V}\big\|_F  \right) \\
    &\leq 2J^2(A^*)\sqrt{T}.
\end{align*}

It remains to suitably bound the $\gamma_1$ and $\gamma_2$ functionals of the image space by the $\gamma$ functionals of the domain. To this end, let
$\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}} := \left\{ (U,V)^\top: U, V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B \right\}$, and define the map $\Phi:\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}} \rightarrow \ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}$, where $\Phi((U,V)^\top = R_{U,V}$, so $\Phi$ is similarly surjective onto $\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}$. Fix $(U_1, V_1)^\top, (U_2,V_2)^\top \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}$. Then, again by the repeated application of the sub-multiplicity of norms and the triangle inequality, we obtain
\begin{align*}
    \big\|{R_{U_1,V_1} - R_{U_2, V_2}}\big\|_2 &= \big\|{\Gamma^\top \check P\left(I_{T-1} \otimes \left\{ \left(U_1^\top V_1 - U_2^\top V_2\right) - (1-p)\operatorname{diag}\left(U_1^\top V_1 - U_2^\top V_2  \right) \right\} \right)P\Gamma}\big\|_2 \\
    &\leq \big\|{\Gamma}\big\|_2^2 \big\|{\check P}\big\|_2^2 \big\|{(U_1^\top V_1 - U_2^\top V_2) - (1-p)\operatorname{diag}(U_1^\top V_1 - U_2^\top V_2) }\big\|_2 \\
    &\leq 2J^2(A^*)\big\|{U_1^\top V_1 - U_2^\top V_2}\big\|_2 = 2J^2(A^*) \big\|{U_1^\top (V_1 - V_2) + (U_1 - U_2)^\top V_2}\big\|_2 \\
    &\leq 2J^2(A^*)\left[\big\|{U_1^\top (V_1 - V_2)}\big\|_2 + \big\|{(U_1 - U_2)^\top V_2}\big\|_2 \right] \\
    &\leq 2J^2(A^*)\left[\big\|{V_1 - V_2}\big\|_2 + \big\|{U_1 - U_2}\big\|_2 \right] \\
    &\leq 4J^2(A^*)\big\|{(U_1, V_1)^\top - (U_2, V_2)^\top}\big\|_2,
\end{align*}
so the map $\Phi$ is $4J^2(A^*)$-Lipschitz with respect to the spectral norm. Employing similar arguments as before, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{R_{U_1, V_1} - R_{U_2,V_2}}\big\|_F \leq 4J^2(A^*)\sqrt{T}\big\|{(U_1 V_1)^\top - (U_2, V_2)^\top}\big\|_F,
\end{equation*}
and thus $\Phi$ is $4J^2(A^*)\sqrt{T}$-Lipschitz with respect to the Frobenius norm. Thus, we have for some absolute constants $C$ (which may differ from line to line)
\begin{equation*}
    \gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2) \leq C J^2(A^*) \gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2) \leq \gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_2),
\end{equation*}
and 
\begin{equation*}
    \gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2) \leq CJ^2(A^*)\sqrt{T}\gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{M}}, \big\|{}\big\|_F) \leq CJ^2(A^*)\sqrt{T}\gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_F) 
\end{equation*}
Gathering facts, we have with probability at least $1 - \delta$, 
\begin{align*}
      \sup_{w \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{R}}} \left|\eta^\top w\eta - \mathbb{E}[\eta^\top w\eta] \right| \leq CJ^2(A^*)\left(\gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_2) + \gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_F)\sqrt{T} + \log(1/\delta) + \sqrt{T\log(1/\delta)} \right),
\end{align*}
which implies that by choosing the learning rate to be $\gamma = (2p^2 TJ^2(A^*)^{-1}$, we have with probability at least $1 - \delta$,
\begin{equation*}
   2\gamma \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \Big\langleV,H_B^{(k)}(\mathbb{E}[M] - M)\Big\rangle \leq
    \frac{C}{p^2}\left(\frac{\gamma_1(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_2)}{T} + \frac{\gamma_2(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B, \big\|{.}\big\|_F)}{\sqrt{T}} + \frac{\log(1/\delta)}{T} + \frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{T}} \right),
\end{equation*}
completing the proof.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:PGD-third-term}
There exist positive constants $C_1, C_2$ such that for $\delta \in (0,1)$, it holds with probability at least $1 - \delta$ that
    \begin{align*}
    &2\gamma\sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \left\{ \big\|{VZ}\big\|_F \big\|{(A^* - B) Z}\big\|_F + (1-p)\big\|{VD}\big\|_F \big\|{(A^* - B)D}\big\|_F 
    + \Big\langleVZ,\widetilde Z - A^* Z\Big\rangle + (1-p)\Big\langleVD,A^* D\Big\rangle \right\} \\
    &\leq \left[\frac{2-p}{p} + \frac{\left\{\overline F_1 + \overline V_1\log(1/\delta)\right\}\left\{\overline c_1 + p(1-p)\overline c_2 \right\}}{p^2 J^2(A^*)T} + \frac{c_3(2-p)\left\{\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F) + \sqrt{\log(1/\delta)} \right\}}{pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T}}  \right] \big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F  \\
     
    &+ \frac{J(A^*)}{p^2}\left[\frac{\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)}{T} + \frac{\log(1/\delta)}{T} + \frac{\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)}{\sqrt{T}} + \sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{T}} \right].
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
Notice that the inner product terms are analogous to the LHS \eqref{eq:dotprod-PGD-unif-bound} with $V$ in place of $A$ (which similarly lies in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B})$. Notice that the RHS of \eqref{eq:dotprod-PGD-unif-bound} does not depend on $A$. Thus, we have 
\begin{align*}
 &\sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}  \Big\langleVZ,\widetilde Z - A^*Z\Big\rangle + (1-p)\Big\langleVD,A^*D\Big\rangle \\ &\leq c_1\left(J^2(A^*)\big\|{A^*}\big\|_2+J(A^*)\right) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)\sqrt{T-1}+\sqrt{(T-1)\log \left(1/\delta\right)}+\log(1/\delta)\right) \\
  
 &\leq c_1' J^3(A^*) \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{1}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_2)+\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F)\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{T\log \left(1/\delta\right)}+\log(1/\delta)\right) \\
 &=: \Psi_0.
\end{align*}


On the other hand, $\big\|{VZ}\big\|_F$ is analogous to \eqref{eq:norm1-unif-bound-PGD}, with $V$ in place of $A$. Thus, we have 
\begin{align*}
    \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{VZ}\big\|_F^2 &\leq pJ^2(A^*)T+ \left(\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right)\left\{\overline c_1 + p(1-p)\overline c_2 \right\}
     \\&+c_3 pJ(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B},\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\right) \\
     &=: \Psi_1.
\end{align*}


Since $x \mapsto \sqrt{x}$ is monotone increasing and continuous over $x \geq 0$, we have that 
\begin{align*}
   \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{VZ}\big\|_F \big\|{(A^*-B)Z}\big\|_F &= \big\|{(A^*-B)Z}\big\|_F\sqrt{\sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}} \big\|{VZ}\big\|_F^2} \\
   &\leq \big\|{(A^* - B)Z}\big\|_F \sqrt{\Psi_1} \\
   &\leq  \sqrt{\Psi_1} \big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F\sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}} \big\|{VZ}\big\|_F  \\
   &\leq \Psi_1 \big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F.
\end{align*}

Proceeding similarly as above, we have by \eqref{eq:norm2-unif-bound-PGD}
\begin{align*}
    (1-p) \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \big\|{VD}\big\|_F^2 &\leq p(1-p) J^2(A^*) T+ \left\{\bar{F}_1+\bar{V}_1\log (1/\delta)\right\}\left(\overline c_1 + p(1-p)^2 \overline c_2\right)\\
&+c_3 p(1-p)J(A^*)\sqrt{T} \left(\ensuremath{\gamma_{2}} \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B,\big\|{\cdot}\big\|_F\right)+\sqrt{\log (1/\delta)}\right) \\
&=: \Psi_2,
\end{align*}
which thus implies
\begin{align*}
    (1-p)\sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \big\|{VD}\big\|_F \big\|{(A^*-B)D}\big\|_F &= \sqrt{1-p}\big\|{(A^*-B)D}\big\|_F\sqrt{1-p} \sqrt{\sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \big\|{VD}\big\|_F^2} \\
    &\leq \sqrt{1-p}\big\|{(A^*-B)D}\big\|_F \sqrt{\Psi_2} \\
    &\leq \sqrt{\Psi_2}\big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F \sqrt{1-p}\sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}\big\|{VD}\big\|_F \\
    &\leq \Psi_2 \big\|{A^* - B}\big\|_F.
\end{align*}
Collecting the $\Psi_0, \Psi_1, \Psi_2$ bounds, grouping terms and substituting $\gamma$ completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem:proj-to-cone}
    Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$ be a closed convex set, and denote $\Pi_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}}$ to be the  projection operator onto the set $\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}$.
Then, it holds that
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{\Pi_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}}(A) - B}\big\|_F \leq \sup_{V \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_B} \Big\langleV,A-B\Big\rangle.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
The proof of Lemma \ref{lem:proj-to-cone} proceeds analogously to the proof of Lemma 2 in \cite{lv2021}, which relies on the construction in Example 2.32 of \cite{mordukhovich2023}.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{lem:isotone-covering-numbers-net}
For any $r, \epsilon > 0$, it holds that 
\begin{equation*}
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \epsilon\right) \leq \ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}, \big\|{.}\big\|_F, r\right) \times \ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}\left(r\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_n, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \epsilon\right).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
Here and in the lemmas that follow, we will denote the Frobenius and spectral balls by $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_{F}$ (which is just $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_n$) and $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_2$ respectively for added clarity.

We will use a double net argument, first to cover $C_{A^*}$ with Frobenius balls, then to cover each residual term with spectral balls. Take a $r$-net $\{H_1, \dots, H_M\}$ with respect to the Frobenius norm, so that for every $H \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}$,  there exists $H_j$ among the centers $H_1, \dots, H_M$ such that $\big\|{H - H_j}\big\|_F \leq r$. Denote the covering number of this net by $M := \ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}, \big\|{.}\big\|_F, r)$, and denote the residual term by $R := H - H_j$. Then $\big\|{H - H_j}\big\|_F \leq r$ is equivalent to saying that $R \in r\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F$.

Now, cover $r\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F$ with spectral balls, consisting of centers $E_1, \dots, E_L$ such that for any $R \in r\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F$, there exists $E_{\ell}$ such that $\big\|{R - E_{\ell}}\big\|_2 \leq \epsilon$. Denote the covering number of this second net by $L := \ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}\left(r\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \epsilon \right)$. By this construction, we have for every $H \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_{A^*}$, there exists $j \in \{1, \dots, M\}$, $\ell \in \{1, \dots, L\}$ (possibly depending on $j$) such that
\begin{equation*}
    \big\|{(H - H_j) - E_{\ell}}\big\|_2 = \big\|{H - (H_j + E_{\ell})}\big\|_2 \leq \epsilon,
\end{equation*}
which means that 
\begin{equation*}
    C_{A^*} \subseteq \cup_{j=1}^M \cup_{\ell=1}^L \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_2(H_j + E_{\ell}, \epsilon).
\end{equation*}
This implies that
\begin{align*}
    &\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}(C_{A^*}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \epsilon) \leq ML,
\end{align*}
which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{lem:metric-entropy-frob-ball}
There exists a constant $C > 0$ such that for every $r > 0$, it holds that
\begin{equation*}
    \int_0^r \log\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}\left(r\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_n, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \epsilon\right) d\epsilon \leq Crn^{3/2}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
It suffices to show that 
\begin{equation*}
    \int_0^1 \log\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_{F}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \varepsilon \right) d\varepsilon \lesssim n^{3/2},
\end{equation*}
since by a change-of-variable $\varepsilon = \epsilon/r$, we have
\begin{equation*}
    \int_0^r \log\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}(r\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \epsilon) d\epsilon = \int_0^r \log\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \frac{\epsilon}{r}\right) d\epsilon 
    = r\int_0^1 \log\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \varepsilon) d\varepsilon.
\end{equation*}
To this end, we will rely on a key entropy number estimate from \cite{hinrichs2017}. In order to make the connection clear, we will translate their results to our special case using their notation. Recall that for the Schatten norms, we have $\big\|{.}\big\|_{S_{\infty}} = \big\|{.}\big\|_2$ and $\big\|{.}\big\|_{S_2} = \big\|{.}\big\|_F$. Then, in their notation, denote $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_p^n := (\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}, \big\|{.}\big\|_{S_p})$, and set $X = \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_2^n = (\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}, \big\|{.}\big\|_F)$, $Y = \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_{\infty}^n = (\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}, \big\|{.}\big\|_2)$. Let $T:X \rightarrow Y$ be the identity operator $T(A) = A$. The respective balls are given by 
\begin{equation*}
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_X = \left\{A \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times n}: \big\|{A}\big\|_F \leq 1 \right\} = \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_{F}, \quad \text{and} \quad \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_Y := \left\{A \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times n}: \big\|{A}\big\|_2 \leq 1 \right\}.
\end{equation*}
The $j$-th entropy number of the operator $T$ of our setup is given by
\begin{equation*}
    e_j(T) := \inf\bigg\{\varepsilon > 0: T(\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_X) = \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F \subseteq \cup_{\ell=1}^{2^{j-1}} (y_{\ell} + \varepsilon \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_Y), 
    \text{ for some } y_1, \dots, y_{2^{j-1}}  \bigg\},
\end{equation*}
which is the smallest achievable radius given $2^{j-1}$ spectral balls to cover the Frobenius ball. With our definition of $T$, $e_j(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_{p}^n \hookrightarrow \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_{q}^n) = e_j(\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^n \hookrightarrow \ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}_{\infty}^n) = e_j(T)$. Using Theorem 1.1 of \cite{hinrichs2017}, we can reverse engineer the covering numbers at different scales $\varepsilon$. 

In particular, the range $n \leq j \leq n^2$ corresponds to $n^{-1/2} \lesssim \varepsilon \lesssim 1$. Moreover, within this range, setting $\varepsilon \asymp e_j(T) \asymp \sqrt{n/j}$ which gives $j \asymp n/\varepsilon^2$, so $2^{Cn/\varepsilon^2}$ balls suffice for a cover, so $\log\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \varepsilon ) \asymp n/\varepsilon^2.$ 

It remains to look at the case $j \geq n^2$. In this regime, we have the range $0 \lesssim \varepsilon \lesssim n^{-1/2}$, where $\varepsilon \asymp n^{-1/2}2^{-j/n^2}$ which gives $j \asymp n^2 \log(C/\varepsilon\sqrt{n})$, so $2^{cn^2\log(C/\varepsilon\sqrt{n})}$ balls suffices as a cover. Thus, $\log\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \varepsilon) \asymp n^2 \log(C/\varepsilon\sqrt{n})$.

Breaking the integral into two parts, we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \int_0^1 \log\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \varepsilon) d\varepsilon  \lesssim  \int_0^{n^{-1/2}} n^2 \log\left(\frac{C}{\varepsilon\sqrt{n}}\right) d\varepsilon 
    + \int_{n^{-1/2}}^1 \frac{n}{\varepsilon^2} d\varepsilon 
    =: I_1 + I_2.
\end{equation*}
 
 
Performing a change of variable with $u = \varepsilon\sqrt{n}$, we obtain
\begin{align*}
    I_1 = n^{3/2}\int_0^1 \log\left(\frac{C}{u}\right) du = n^{3/2}\left(1 + \log(C) \right).
\end{align*}
On the other hand, we have $I_2 = n^{3/2} - n$,
                
so 
\begin{equation*}
    \int_0^1 \log\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}_F, \big\|{.}\big\|_2, \varepsilon \right) d\varepsilon \lesssim n^{3/2},
\end{equation*}
as desired.
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- corollary-5: body_md -->
\label{cor:stat-lipschitz-regression}
There exist constants $c_1,c_2>1$ such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, if 
\begin{equation*}
T \geq c_1\frac{J^4(A^*)}{p^4}\max\bigg\{n\log n, \log^{2} \left(1/\delta\right),\frac{p^{2/3} n^{4/3} L^{2/3}}{J^{2/3}(A^*)}, \frac{p^{1/2} n^{3/2} L^{1/2} \log^{1/2} n}{J^{1/2}(A^*)}\bigg\},
\end{equation*}
 
then it holds with probability at least $1-\delta$ that
 
 
\begin{align*}
\big\|{\hat{A}-A^*}\big\|_F &\leq c_2 \frac{J^3(A^*)}{p^2}\bigg(\frac{ n^{3/2}\log^{3/2}(n)}{T}+\frac{n^{1/2}\log^{1/2}(n)}{\sqrt{T}} \\
&+ \frac{n L^{1/3} \log^{1/3}(n)}{ T^{2/3} }  \max\left\{{ \frac{(n \log n)^{2/3} }{T^{1/3}},1}\right\} + \frac{\sqrt{\log \left(1/\delta\right)}}{\sqrt{T}}\bigg).
\end{align*}
<!-- end corollary-5 -->
<!-- remark-15: body_md -->
For $p = 1$, we recover the statement of Corollary 4 of \cite{tyagi2024learning} up to constants and a $J(A^*)$ factor.
<!-- end remark-15 -->
<!-- corollary-6: body_md -->
\label{cor:pgd-lipschitz-regression}
Suppose that the learning rate is chosen as $\gamma = \frac{1}{2p^2 T J^2(A^*) }$. Then there exist constants $c,C,C' > 1$ such that for any $\delta \in (0,1)$, if 
\begin{align*}
        T &\geq C'\frac{J^4(A^*)}{p^4}\max\left\{n\log n, \log^{2} \left(1/\delta\right), \frac{p^{2/3} n^{4/3} L^{2/3}}{J^{2/3}(A^*)},\frac{p^{1/2} n^{3/2} L^{1/2} \log^{1/2} n}{J^{1/2}(A^*)}\right\}\\
        &+C'\frac{J^2(A^*)}{p^2}\max\left\{n^{3/2}\log^{3/2} n, \log \left(1/\delta\right),\frac{p^{1/3} n^{5/3} L^{1/3}}{J^{1/3}(A^*)}, \frac{p^{2/7} n^{12/7} L^{2/7} \log^{8/7} n}{J^{2/7}(A^*)}\right\},
    \end{align*} 
then with probability at least $1 - \delta$, for all $k = 0,1,\dots$, it holds that
    \begin{align*}
        & \big\|{A^{(k)}-A^*}\big\|_F \le \rho^k \big\|{A^{(0)}-A^*}\big\|_F +\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\rho}
                                                                                                       
    \end{align*}
where $\rho \le 1-\frac{1}{cJ^2(A^*)}$ and
\begin{equation*}
    \varepsilon =C\frac{J(A^*)}{p^2} \bigg(\max\left\{{\frac{ n^{3/2}\log^{3/2} n}{T},\frac{n^{1/2}\log^{1/2} n}{\sqrt{T}}}\right\} + \frac{ n (L \log n)^{1/3}}{ T^{2/3} }  \max\left\{{ \frac{(n \log n)^{2/3} }{T^{1/3}},1}\right\} + \frac{\sqrt{\log \left(1/\delta\right)}}{\sqrt{T}}
\bigg).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end corollary-6 -->
<!-- remark-16: body_md -->
In Corollary \ref{cor:stat-lipschitz-regression}, the sharp boundary for $\big\|{\widehat A - A^*}\big\|_F = o(1)$ when $p = o(1)$ is a long and ugly expression. However, it is easy to see that for a fixed level $\delta$, a sufficient scaling regime is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:p-floor-lipschitz}
    J^{3/2}(A^*)\max\left\{1, L^{1/6} \frac{n^{5/6}\log^{3/4}(n)}{T^{1/4}}  \right\} = o(p),
\end{equation}
with $p = o(1)$. 
Then, we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \limsup_{k \rightarrow \infty} \big\|{A^{(k)} - A^*}\big\|_F \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{1 - \rho}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end remark-16 -->
<!-- remark-17: body_md -->
For the projection step in Lipschitz regression, each row projection is a convex-quadratic program with $n$ variables and $O(n)$ linear constraints. This can be solved, e.g., using interior-point methods with an overall running time polynomial in $n$.
<!-- end remark-17 -->
